# MEF Platform — Build Status

Source: `/docs/INVENTORY.md` (file-level inventory + Overlaps analysis), plus a small number of targeted follow-up reads cited inline where they go beyond what INVENTORY.md already established. Scope is bounded to the assessment / onboarding / welcome / cross-assessment-intelligence subsystem, which is what INVENTORY.md documented in depth — this is not a build-status audit of Food Lens, Movement, Wearables, etc. as standalone features. No application code was written or changed to produce this document.

---

## Direction of travel

**`lib/assessment-runtime/` + `lib/assessment-foundation/` (the Unified Adaptive Assessment Runtime) is the chosen shared foundation for all assessments going forward.** The older, per-assessment engines are legacy — candidates to be migrated onto the unified runtime over time, not systems to keep building out in parallel indefinitely.

This is not an inference — it's what the schema's own migration states. `supabase/migrations/00000000000098_unified_assessment_foundation.sql`'s header comment: *"a reusable question/section/assessment schema that future assessments (Four Doctors v2, HAQ, WBSA, Breathing, Nutrition, Primal Pattern, etc.) can opt into instead of inventing a new bespoke table set each time."* It explicitly names Four Doctors, HAQ, and Primal Pattern — three of today's legacy engines — as intended future migrants onto this schema, not permanent parallel systems.

- **Schema**: `unified_assessment_definitions`, `unified_assessment_sections`, `unified_assessment_questions` (`supabase/migrations/00000000000098_unified_assessment_foundation.sql`), `unified_assessment_sessions`, `unified_assessment_answers` (`supabase/migrations/00000000000099_unified_assessment_runtime.sql`).
- **Logic**: `lib/assessment-foundation/{repository,adaptive,index}.ts` (content read layer), `lib/assessment-runtime/{data,conditions,session,findings,types,index}.ts` (session/answer/branching/findings engine).
- **First (and, as of this document, only) adopter**: WBSA — content seeded by `supabase/migrations/00000000000100_wbsa_schema_extensions.sql` and `00000000000101_wbsa_content.sql`, served by `components/wbsa/WbsaTaker.tsx` and `app/assessments/wbsa/{page,take/page,results/[sessionId]/page}.tsx`.
- **Not yet migrated**: the generic points-scored engine (CHEK HLC1 / Four Doctors / Short-HAQ), Primal Pattern, Body Assessment, and the Onboarding adaptive engine — see 🟡 below for each.

---

## ✅ Built & Complete

Features where schema, logic, UI, and a real data path all exist and connect, verified by file citation.

### 1. WBSA (Whole-Body Systems Assessment) — end-to-end on the new foundation
The one assessment that is both feature-complete *and* already on the chosen target architecture.
- **Schema**: `unified_assessment_*` tables above, plus WBSA-specific extensions (`allows_prefer_not_to_answer` column, 7 new `registry_entries.domain` values) in migration 100; 16 sections/questions seeded by migration 101.
- **Logic**: `lib/assessment-runtime/*`, `lib/assessment-foundation/*`, `lib/wbsa/{safety,comparison,copy,constants,results}.ts`.
- **Server actions**: `app/actions/wbsa.ts`.
- **UI**: `components/wbsa/{WbsaTaker,WbsaQuestionCard}.tsx`; member routes `app/assessments/wbsa/page.tsx`, `.../take/page.tsx`, `.../results/[sessionId]/page.tsx`; coach route `app/coach/clients/[id]/wbsa/[sessionId]/page.tsx`.
- **Registered**: `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts`'s `WBSA` entry (`key: 'wbsa'`, `implementationStatus: 'live'`), backed by the `assessment_definitions` catalog row inserted in migration 101.

### 2. Universal Health Finding Registry — cross-assessment findings aggregation
A single findings table fed by 7 independent adapters, consumed widely by the intelligence layer.
- **Schema**: `registry_entries` (`supabase/migrations/00000000000040_universal_health_registry.sql`).
- **Logic**: `lib/registry/{data,trendStatus,types,timeline}.ts` + 9 adapter files in `lib/registry/adapters/` (`bodyAssessment`, `coachIntelligence`, `foodLens`, `foodProducts`, `movement`, `onboarding`, `primalPattern`, `unifiedAssessment`, `wearables`, `questionnaireEngine`).
- **Consumers**: `lib/intelligence-engine/registryFindings.ts`, `lib/intelligence-engine/crossAssessmentCorrelations.ts`, `lib/reassessment-intelligence/service.ts`, `lib/root-map/*` (per INVENTORY.md's Overlaps §Item 8 Resolved import lists).

### 3. Questionnaire catalog + membership/access gating
The browse/filter/resume/gate experience itself is complete, independent of which underlying engine each listed assessment runs on.
- **Schema**: `assessment_definitions`, `membership_tiers`.
- **Logic**: `lib/assessment-registry/{registry,access,status,membership,versionLock}.ts`, `app/actions/questionnaireCatalog.ts`.
- **UI**: `app/questionnaires/page.tsx`, `components/questionnaires/{CatalogQuestionnaireCard,QuestionnaireCatalogView,QuestionnairesHomeCard}.tsx`.

### 4. Assessment Assignments (coach assigns a questionnaire to a member)
- **Schema**: `assessment_assignments` (`supabase/migrations/00000000000077_assessment_assignments.sql`).
- **Logic**: `app/actions/assessmentAssignments.ts`.
- **UI**: `app/coach/clients/[id]/AssessmentAssignmentPanel.tsx`.

### 5. Reassessment Intelligence trigger system
Decides *when* to suggest a reassessment (worsening registry findings, elapsed time, experiment outcomes, recommendation sequences) — a real writer for a table that previously had none.
- **Schema**: `reassessment_schedules`, `reassessment_schedule_configs`.
- **Logic**: `lib/reassessment-intelligence/{data,service}.ts`.
- **Consumers**: `app/actions/{longitudinalIntelligence,rootCauseSignals,rootMap}.ts`, `app/api/cron/daily-coaching-scan/route.ts`.
- **UI surface**: `app/profile/reassessments/{page,new/page,[id]/page}.tsx`.

### 6. One consolidated assessment registry (was two)
`lib/assessments/registry.ts` (the "Reusable Assessment Engine" content-mapping registry — Questionnaire + Copy pairs for CHEK HLC1, Four Doctors, Short-HAQ) has been absorbed into `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts`, which is now the single registry for all 9 assessment keys. `lib/assessments/registry.ts` is deleted.
- **What changed**: `QUESTIONNAIRE_CONTENT_REGISTRY` (content map, keyed by `questionnaire.id`) now lives in `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts` alongside the pre-existing `ASSESSMENT_REGISTRY` (catalog map, keyed by `AssessmentKey`) — two distinct maps in one file, not merged into one shape, since their `AssessmentDefinition` types name unrelated things (disambiguated in-file via the `AssessmentContentDefinition` import alias). Exported functions (`getAssessmentDefinition`, `findAssessmentDefinition`, `listAssessmentDefinitions`) kept their exact names and signatures.
- **Callers updated**: `app/actions/assessments.ts`, `app/assessments/[questionnaireId]/history/page.tsx`, `lib/coaching-insights/sources/assessmentSource.ts`, and 4 test files (`tests/{assessments-engine,assessments-isolation,assessments-lifecycle-integration,short-haq-engine}.test.ts`) — all now import from `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts`. `tests/assessments-isolation.test.ts`'s static-scan guard (which asserts only one file is allowed to reference "Four Doctors" outside its own module) was updated to point at the new location; the isolation property itself is unchanged since the new location sits outside that test's scanned directory set.
- **Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run lint` — 0 errors (29 pre-existing warnings, all in files this change didn't touch); `npm run build` (`next build`) — compiled successfully, all routes generated including every `/assessment*` and `/assessments/*` route, no build errors; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing across 165 test files, including the real-DB integration suite (`tests/assessments-lifecycle-integration.test.ts`) against a live local Supabase instance. No behavior change for any of the 9 assessments.
- **Deployed**: commit `e6318fb` pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`) and built/deployed to the `mef-platform` Vercel project's Production environment, aliased to `app.mefwellness.com`. Confirmed via Vercel build logs that the deployment cloned `Branch: main, Commit: e6318fb` and completed ("Build Completed" / "Deployment completed" / status Ready). Confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com` is live and serving from this deployment (`server: Vercel`, 307 redirect to `/login` for an unauthenticated request, matching `app/page.tsx`'s expected routing). Not verified: any actual page content or the 9 assessments' behavior in a browser — see the click-by-click checklist delivered with this task.

## 🟡 Built but Incomplete

### 1. Generic points-scored questionnaire engine (CHEK HLC1, Four Doctors, Short-HAQ)
- **What exists**: fully working end-to-end on its own tables — `wellness_assessments`/`wellness_assessment_answers`/`wellness_assessment_category_scores`; logic in `lib/assessments/engine/{navigation,scoring,types}.ts` + `lib/assessments/store.ts`; content in `lib/assessments/{chek-hlc1,four-doctors,short-haq}/{index.ts,questionnaire.json,copy.ts}`; UI in `components/assessments/AssessmentTaker.tsx` + `app/assessments/[questionnaireId]/*`.
- **What's unfinished**: not migrated onto `unified_assessment_*`/`lib/assessment-runtime`. Three separate live assessments still depend on a table set and engine migration 98's own comment names as a pre-unification system. No migration path or dual-write has been started.

### 2. Primal Pattern Diet Type
- **What exists**: `lib/primal-pattern/{questionnaire,scoring,store}.ts`, `primal_pattern_assessments`/`primal_pattern_assessment_answers`/`primal_pattern_profiles` tables, `components/primal-pattern/PrimalPatternTaker.tsx`, `app/assessments/primal-pattern-diet-type/*`.
- **What's unfinished**: also named explicitly in migration 98's comment as an intended future migrant to the unified runtime; still on its own bespoke tables/engine, unmigrated.

### 3. Body Assessment (camera-based posture/movement capture)
- **What exists**: `lib/body-assessment/*` (18 files), `body_assessments`/`body_assessment_captures`/`body_assessment_findings`/`body_assessment_coach_reviews`/etc. tables, `components/body-assessment/AssessmentWizard.tsx`, `app/assessment/*` + coach review routes under `app/coach/clients/[id]/body-assessments/*` and `app/coach/review-queue/*`.
- **What's unfinished**: not on the unified runtime. Architecturally distinct from the other four (media capture + coach review, not question/answer), so a literal migration onto `unified_assessment_questions` may not even be the right target — whether Body Assessment is in scope for unification at all is itself unresolved (not stated either way in migration 98's comment or anywhere else found).

### 4. Onboarding adaptive engine
- **What exists**: real per-concern adaptive selection — `lib/onboarding/adaptivePlan.ts` + `lib/adaptive-assessment-engine/{select,types}.ts`, 9 concern banks in `lib/onboarding/concernBanks/*`, served by `app/onboarding/{OnboardingForm,OnboardingFlow}.tsx`, stored in `onboarding_questions`/`onboarding_submissions`/`onboarding_answers`.
- **What's unfinished**: two things. (a) Not on the unified runtime, same as the other three. (b) Internally, onboarding itself still runs two different question-ordering systems on the same `onboarding_questions` table: `lib/onboarding/branching.ts` (legacy reorder-only, now used only by the reassessment flow at `app/profile/reassessments/*`) alongside `lib/onboarding/adaptivePlan.ts` (the real adaptive engine, used by live `/onboarding`) — differentiated only by the `question_pool` column added in migration `00000000000097_onboarding_adaptive_engine.sql`. This split was never resolved into one system; per that migration's own comment, the reassessment flow "deliberately keeps its original behavior unchanged" rather than adopting the adaptive engine.

### 5. ~~Two assessment registries~~ — resolved, see ✅ Built & Complete #6

### 6. Two coexisting welcome/first-run flows
- **What exists**: `app/welcome/{page,WelcomeFlow}.tsx` + `lib/welcome/{eligibility,goals}.ts` (four-screen welcome experience, goal selection) sits *in front of* the pre-existing `app/onboarding/{page,OnboardingIntro,ConsentForm,OnboardingFlow,OnboardingForm}.tsx` consent → onboarding progression, gated by `WELCOME_FLOW_ENABLED = true` (`lib/welcome/eligibility.ts`) and a per-profile `welcome_flow_eligible` flag set only for new signups (migration 85's `handle_new_user` trigger).
- **What's unfinished**: confirmed by reading `app/actions/welcome.ts` — `completeWelcomeFlow()` marks `welcome_flow_completed_at` and simply `redirect('/')`s back to the routing hub (`app/page.tsx`), which then still runs its own separate `hasCompletedConsent()` / `onboarding_submissions` check and sends a brand-new member on to `/onboarding` next. The welcome flow does not replace or absorb consent/onboarding — it's an additive step in front of it for members marked eligible at signup. Existing members (created before migration 85, or otherwise ineligible) skip welcome entirely and go straight through the original consent → onboarding path — so two first-run sequences exist by population, not as alternatives a single member ever chooses between. See "Open decisions" below.

### 7. Two Four Doctors results routes
- **What exists**: the generic `app/assessments/[questionnaireId]/results/[assessmentId]/page.tsx` (serves CHEK HLC1, Four Doctors, Short-HAQ per the registry) alongside a dedicated `app/assessments/four-doctors/results/[assessmentId]/page.tsx`.
- **What's unfinished**: not actually ambiguous at runtime (see "Open decisions" — this is resolved by Next.js routing precedence, confirmed by both routes' own code), but it is unfinished cleanup: the generic route's Four Doctors code path is effectively dead (Next.js's static-over-dynamic resolution means `/assessments/four-doctors/results/*` never reaches the generic handler), yet no comment, redirect, or dead-code marker in the generic route acknowledges that Four Doctors is excluded from it in practice. A reader of only `app/assessments/[questionnaireId]/results/[assessmentId]/page.tsx` would not know this.

### 8. Unified comparison vocabulary (`lib/assessment-comparison/`) — single adopter
- **What exists**: `lib/assessment-comparison/{adapters,types,classify,index}.ts` — a shared `ComparisonDirection`/classification vocabulary intended to normalize "improved/declined" language across assessment types.
- **What's unfinished**: as of this document, its only importer found via `grep` is `lib/wbsa/comparison.ts`. The CHEK HLC1/Four Doctors/Short-HAQ engine (`lib/assessments/comparison.ts`) and Onboarding (`lib/onboarding/comparison.ts`) each still have their own separate, pre-existing comparison logic that does not go through this shared vocabulary. "Unified" describes the intent, not yet the adoption.

## ❌ Not Built

Referenced in the codebase or migrations as intended, with no implementation found.

- **`readiness-to-change` and `finding-1-love` assessments** — both are `comingSoon()` placeholder entries in `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts` with `implementationStatus: 'coming_soon'`, `componentRef: 'n/a — not yet implemented'`, no questionnaire content, and `scoringAdapter: 'none'`. Catalog rows exist (per that file's comment, from `supabase/migrations/00000000000078_coming_soon_assessments.sql`) but no content, route, or scoring logic exists anywhere.
- **Real AI/LLM provider wiring for most subsystems** — confirmed by reading two registries directly: `lib/ai/providers/registry.ts` registers all 4 named providers (`openai`, `anthropic`, `google`, `local`) as `UnconfiguredProvider` stubs whose methods throw rather than call a real API; `lib/coach-intelligence/providers/registry.ts` (Coach Intelligence Workspace — AI analysis of a submitted Body Assessment) does the same for all 3 of its named providers (`openai_gpt4`, `anthropic_claude`, `custom_model`) — every call returns/throws `"... is not configured"` by design. The same `UnconfiguredProvider`/`not_configured` naming pattern also appears (found via `grep`, files not individually opened to confirm) in `lib/food-lens/providers/registry.ts`, `lib/food-lens/providers/labelOcr/registry.ts`, `lib/body-assessment/providers/registry.ts`, `lib/wearables/providers/registry.ts`, and `lib/movement/providers/registry.ts` — **not individually verified, marked as such.**
  - Exception found: `lib/ai/providers/anthropic.ts` is a real `AnthropicProvider` implementation, and `lib/conversation-coach/provider.ts` does call `registerProvider('anthropic', real)` to wire it into the registry for Conversation Coach specifically — but only when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` env vars are both set. Checked `.env.local` in this repo: both are present as empty (unset) values. **Not verified** whether they're set in the actual deployed/production environment — this only confirms local repo state.
- **`services/knowledge-engine-api/` and `services/pattern-prioritization-engine/`** — both are declared workspace members (root `package.json`'s `"workspaces": ["apps/*", "services/*", "packages/*"]`) but each contains only `README.md` + `package.json`; no `src/` or any other source file exists in either directory.
- **`packages/mef-method-repository/`** — contains only `README.md` and an empty `content/.gitkeep`; no actual method/content files exist despite being a declared workspace package.
- **Reserved coaching-insights data sources** — `lib/coaching-insights/types.ts` declares a `CoachingSourceId` union that includes Sleep, Stress, Blood Work, Wearables, and Movement Assessments as named source ids, with its own comment stating "only the first five are backed by a real provider today" (check-ins, Food Lens, assessments, progress, and one more — see `lib/coaching-insights/sources/registry.ts`'s `ACTIVE_PROVIDERS`). No provider file exists for the 5 reserved-but-unimplemented sources.

## ⚠️ Not verified in the running app

Everything above is verified from files only — no dev server was started and no browser was used to produce this document. To confirm the claims above, the following would need to be clicked through:

- **WBSA take → results flow**: start a WBSA assessment at `/assessments/wbsa/take`, answer through all 16 sections, and confirm a session actually persists, findings compute, and `/assessments/wbsa/results/[sessionId]` renders without error. Code exists end-to-end; it has not been exercised.
- **Four Doctors results routing**: complete a Four Doctors assessment and click "View My Results" to confirm the dedicated `app/assessments/four-doctors/results/[assessmentId]/page.tsx` actually renders (per its own comment's Next.js static-over-dynamic routing claim), not the generic results page.
- **Welcome flow gating**: create a brand-new signup and confirm `/welcome` actually appears (i.e., `handle_new_user` from migration 85 is correctly setting `welcome_flow_eligible = true` in the live database), and that completing it correctly falls through to `/onboarding` next, not a loop or dead end.
- **Onboarding vs. reassessment question order**: take `/onboarding` fresh vs. starting a reassessment at `/profile/reassessments/new`, to confirm the two really do use visibly different question-selection behavior (adaptive concern banks vs. fixed reorder) as the code claims.
- **Coach Intelligence "not configured" behavior**: as a coach, trigger "Run analysis" on a submitted Body Assessment and confirm the UI shows a clear, handled message rather than a raw thrown error or a broken page.
- **Coming-soon assessment cards**: confirm `readiness-to-change` and `finding-1-love` render as non-clickable/clearly-labeled cards on `/questionnaires` rather than as dead links.
- **Database migration state**: whether all 101 migrations (including the `assessment_definitions` catalog rows whose UUIDs must match `lib/assessment-registry/registry.ts` exactly per that file's own comment) have actually been applied to the live/production Supabase instance was not checked — only the migration files themselves were read, not the database.
- **Production environment variables**: whether `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (Conversation Coach) or any other provider credentials are set in the deployed environment (Vercel) — only the local `.env.local` checked into this working copy was inspected.

## Open decisions

**1. Which Four Doctors results route is canonical?**
Resolved in code, but the underlying question of *whether this is the intended long-term shape* is still open. `app/assessments/four-doctors/results/[assessmentId]/page.tsx`'s own header comment states it's "a static route... Next.js resolves ahead of the generic dynamic `app/assessments/[questionnaireId]/results/[assessmentId]/` route for this one exact path" — and `components/assessments/AssessmentTaker.tsx` (line ~301, ~322) redirects to `/assessments/${slug}/results/${id}`, which for the Four Doctors slug lands on that dedicated route by Next.js's static-over-dynamic precedence. So **the dedicated route is canonical today, by routing mechanics, not by an explicit decision recorded anywhere.** The generic route remains canonical for CHEK HLC1 and Short-HAQ only. Open question: should the generic `[questionnaireId]/results` route be updated to explicitly acknowledge it never serves Four Doctors (dead-code clarity), or should Four Doctors eventually be folded back into the generic route like its two siblings?

**2. Does the welcome flow replace the onboarding-consent path, or stay conditional?**
Confirmed conditional, not a replacement, by reading `app/actions/welcome.ts`: `completeWelcomeFlow()` redirects to `/` and lets `app/page.tsx`'s existing `hasCompletedConsent()` / `onboarding_submissions` checks run exactly as before — a brand-new member goes welcome → (back through the hub) → onboarding, not welcome-instead-of-onboarding. Gating is by `welcome_flow_eligible`, set only for new signups (migration 85), so existing members never see `/welcome` at all and go straight to consent/onboarding as before. Open question: is a permanent two-population split (new members: welcome + onboarding both; existing members: onboarding only) the intended end state, or is welcome meant to eventually absorb what `app/onboarding/{OnboardingIntro,ConsentForm}.tsx` currently do for new members — at which point those two files would become dead code for the new-member path (still load-bearing for the existing-member path, since they're not eligibility-gated the way welcome is)? Nothing in the code states an intended end state either way.

## Home dashboard redesign (2026-07-25)

Visual and structural redesign of the member Home screen (`app/dashboard/page.tsx` and its components) — no section removed, no copy changed, no data source changed. Coach-facing routes/components untouched.

- **Hero**: `components/dashboard/HomeHero.tsx` — full-bleed photo (`public/images/home-hero.jpg`) replaces the old plain header + white Root Score card. Dark diagonal gradient (stronger left/top) for legibility; logo and avatar sit on a subtle dark scrim in the top corners. Root Score counts up on load via `components/dashboard/RootScoreCountUp.tsx`. Before a member's first check-in, the hero shows only the greeting (`hasCheckins` prop) so it doesn't compete with `FirstCheckInWelcome` below it — matches the original page's own gating, just relocated.
- **Rhythm**: sections are grouped into six labeled zones (Quick Actions, Today, Your Path, What Root Is Noticing, Trends, Coming Up), each fading/rising into view on scroll (`components/dashboard/RevealOnScroll.tsx`, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`). No two consecutive sections repeat the same treatment — full-bleed color panels, plain divider rows, a horizontal carousel, CSS image-backed cards, and white cards (now the minority) are rotated deliberately.
- **Quick Actions** (`components/dashboard/QuickActionsCarousel.tsx`): Movement/Food Lens/Progress/Flag a Concern as one horizontal carousel. `components/checkin/ConcernFlag.tsx` is now a controlled component (`open`/`onOpenChange`) triggered by the carousel tile instead of its own internal button — same `flagConcern()` action, same copy. `components/DashboardQuickLinks.tsx` (superseded, no remaining importers) was deleted.
- **Questionnaires** (`components/questionnaires/QuestionnairesHomeCard.tsx`): now a plain row with a real progress bar, no card.
- **Guided Posture & Movement Assessment** (`components/MovementAssessmentCard.tsx`): new `variant="imageBacked"` (CSS-only gradient + silhouette treatment — no real photo asset exists for this yet) used only by the dashboard; `variant="card"` (default, unchanged) still serves `/today`, so that page's appearance is untouched.
- **Daily Wellness / Assigned Programs**: `components/checkin/DailyWellnessSection.tsx` is now a flat tinted panel; `components/AssignedProgramsCard.tsx` is now a plain divider row.
- **Unlock Smarter Coaching** (`components/wearables/ConnectWearableCard.tsx`, `dashboard` variant only): now a full-bleed dark green panel edge-to-edge with the site's own padding/rail offsets; the `today` variant (currently unused elsewhere) is untouched.
- **What Root Is Noticing**: `WhatWereNoticingCard`, `RootMapCard`, `CoachingMessageCard`, `RecommendationsCard` are visually grouped inside one tinted panel in `app/dashboard/page.tsx`; each component file is unmodified and keeps its own independent Suspense boundary/fetch.
- **Energy Trend**: the shared `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx` is also used by the coach client view (`app/coach/clients/[id]/page.tsx`) and was left completely unmodified. The "line draws in" animation instead lives in a new dashboard-only wrapper, `components/dashboard/AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`, which wipes the unmodified chart in via `clip-path` on mount.
- **RootScoreCard.tsx** (superseded by `HomeHero.tsx`, no remaining importers) was deleted.
- New shared CSS in `app/globals.css`: `.mef-scrollbar-hidden` (carousels), `.mef-press` (button/link press state, all respecting `prefers-reduced-motion`).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run lint` — 0 errors (only pre-existing warnings in unrelated files); full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing across 165 test files; `npm run build` (`next build`) — compiled successfully, all 64 routes generated including `/dashboard` and every `/coach/*` route. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase, logged in as the seeded `member.one@example.test` dev account): hero, count-up score, Quick Actions carousel, the Flag a Concern reveal panel (typed text, Send button enabled — not submitted), the image-backed Movement Assessment card, the tinted Noticing zone, the Energy Trend chart, the quiet "Next session" row, and the full-bleed Unlock Smarter Coaching panel all rendered as designed with zero browser console errors.

**Not verified**: the pre-first-check-in hero state (greeting-only) and the "building your Root Score" (no-score-yet) hero state were reasoned through in code but not exercised against a real zero-checkin or zero-score account in this pass. Not deployed — this work is committed to a local branch only as of this entry; no push/deploy has happened yet.

**2026-07-25 follow-up — pre-first-check-in state fixes** (found on the live site after the above shipped):

- **Double greeting**: `components/FirstCheckInWelcome.tsx`'s headline changed from "Welcome, {firstName}" (a second greeting, duplicating the hero's "Good evening, {firstName}") to "Let's get started" — forward-looking, no name. The component no longer takes a `firstName` prop at all (nothing in it used it anymore); its only two callers, `app/dashboard/page.tsx` and `app/today/page.tsx` (this component is intentionally shared between both, per its own doc comment), were updated to drop the now-unused prop.
- **Hero height**: `components/dashboard/HomeHero.tsx`'s `HeroChrome` gained a `compact` mode, used only when `hasCheckins` is false — no forced `min-height`, no `mt-auto` push-to-bottom, just the header row plus a short gap before the greeting, so the band is only as tall as its content actually needs. The scored/full hero (`hasCheckins` true) is completely unchanged — verified side by side in a browser (screenshot before/after match).
- **CTA hidden behind the bottom nav / overlapping the floating chat button**: was real, and reproducible — measured via Playwright bounding boxes at 375×667 (iPhone SE, this app's narrowest supported width): the CTA button and the floating "Ask Root" launcher overlapped by roughly 10×37px, and the CTA's bottom edge was 2px past the bottom nav's top edge. Fixed by the hero-height change above plus tightening `FirstCheckInWelcome`'s own internal padding/margins and the page's hero-to-card gap (`app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `pt-8` → `pt-4`). Re-measured after the fix at the same 375×667 viewport: CTA bottom now sits 64px clear of the floating button's top edge and 128px clear of the bottom nav's top edge.
- **General bottom-padding safety net**: `<main>`'s mobile bottom padding in `app/dashboard/page.tsx` increased `pb-28` → `pb-32`, applying to both dashboard states (not just pre-check-in), per the instruction that no content should ever be trapped under the bottom nav on this page.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; targeted `eslint` on every touched file clean; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing (one earlier run in this session showed 2 failures in real-DB integration tests unrelated to any file touched here — `movement-profile`/`pattern-prioritization`-style tests — that passed cleanly on an immediate rerun, consistent with known flakiness in those real-Postgres-backed suites, not a regression); `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase) logged in as the seeded **zero-check-in** dev account (`member.two@example.test`) at both 375×667 and 390×844 — single greeting, "Let's get started" card, CTA fully visible with no scrolling, no overlap with the floating chat button or bottom nav, zero console errors at either size. Also re-verified the scored-hero state (`member.one@example.test`) renders identically to before. Not deployed as of this entry.

**2026-07-25 second follow-up — hero was too short, Quick Actions became compact pill chips:**

- **Hero size**: the previous fix (above) had gone too far — a compact, content-sized hero read as a "thin dark strip" rather than an intentional photographic hero, and left a large unused gap between the welcome card and the bottom nav. `HeroChrome`'s `compact` mode now uses `min-h-[32vh]` (roughly a third of the viewport, per the request's "30–35%") with `mt-auto` restored so the greeting sits with real breathing room below the header, instead of a fixed small gap. Measured at exactly 375×667 (this app's narrowest supported width) via Playwright: hero renders at 213px = **32.0% of viewport height**, landing inside the requested range.
- **Hard requirement re-verified after growing the hero**: measured the "Complete your first check-in" CTA's actual bounding box against the floating "Ask Root" launcher and the bottom nav at 375×667. At `min-h-[34vh]` the CTA cleared the floating button by only 0.5px — too fragile — so the value was dialed back to `32vh`, which measures a **13.8px clear gap** between the CTA's bottom edge and the floating button's top edge, and **78.3px** clear of the bottom nav, with zero scrolling needed to reach the button. `app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s hero-to-card gap was also tightened (`pt-4` → `pt-3`) to recover a little more room for the taller hero without moving the CTA down. The scored-hero state (`hasCheckins` true) was not touched.
- **Quick Actions redesigned as pill chips**: `components/dashboard/QuickActionsCarousel.tsx`'s four tiles (Movement, Food Lens, Progress, Flag a Concern) went from card-sized tiles (rounded-24, padding, icon circle, label, subtitle) to single-line rounded pills — small inline icon, label only, no subtitle, tinted `bg-[#1B3A2D]/[0.06]` background with deep-green text, sized to roughly one line of text plus padding. Same hrefs, same `flagConcern()` action and reveal behavior, same horizontal scroll — only the visual weight changed. Because the zone is now much shorter, `app/dashboard/page.tsx` dropped the page's blanket `space-y-14`/`space-y-20` zone spacing in favor of per-zone margins, with the gap specifically after Quick Actions (before Today) tightened to `mt-8`/`mt-10` while every other zone-to-zone gap keeps the original `mt-14`/`mt-20`.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; targeted `eslint` on every touched file clean; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing on a clean run (an interleaved run again showed the same 2 known-flaky real-DB integration tests, unrelated to these files, passing on immediate rerun); `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright): the zero-check-in state at exactly 375×667 with the measurements above, and the pill chips (including the Flag a Concern reveal panel) at 390×844 logged in as `member.one@example.test`. Zero console errors in either case. Not deployed as of this entry.

**2026-07-25 third follow-up — profile menu regression (2 compounding CSS bugs, no content actually broke):**

Tapping the avatar opened a clipped white sliver showing only the Sign Out icon, cut off at the top of the screen. `ProfileSheet.tsx` itself was never touched by the redesign and its 6 menu items + header + Sign Out were confirmed still present in the DOM the whole time (`nav[aria-label="Profile"] a` always returned 6) — this was purely a CSS regression in `components/dashboard/HomeHero.tsx`'s new hero chrome, from two compounding bugs:

1. **Wrong containing block**: the avatar's new "subtle scrim" wrapper (`<div className="rounded-full bg-black/25 p-1 backdrop-blur-sm">`) used `backdrop-filter` (Tailwind's `backdrop-blur-sm`), which — like `transform`/`filter`/`perspective` — creates a new CSS containing block for `position: fixed` descendants. `ProfileSheet`'s `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0` sheet was therefore sizing and positioning itself against that ~48px scrim box instead of the viewport, then getting clipped by the hero section's `overflow-hidden`. Measured via Playwright before the fix: the sheet's bounding box was `x:307, y:-496, width:48, height:579` — a 48px-wide box starting almost 500px above the viewport. Fix: dropped `backdrop-blur-sm` from both the avatar and logo scrim wrappers (kept a plain, slightly stronger `bg-black/40` tint — no filter needed since the avatar button is already an opaque white circle and doesn't strictly need a scrim at all).
2. **Wrong stacking order** (found only after fixing #1 — the sheet was correctly positioned but the bottom nav and floating chat button still visually painted on top of it): the hero's content wrapper had `z-10`, which — combined with `position: relative` — establishes its own stacking context. Any `position: fixed` descendant nested inside it (the sheet is nested three levels deep, via `AvatarLink`) has its z-index compared only *within* that local z-10 context, not against page-root siblings like `BottomNav` (`z-20`). The whole z-10 subtree, as one unit, was out-ranked by `BottomNav` at the page's root stacking context, regardless of the sheet's own `z-50`. Fix: removed `z-10` from that wrapper. It wasn't actually needed — the wrapper is still `position: relative` and comes after the hero's `absolute`-positioned image/gradient layers in DOM order, which alone is enough to paint it on top of them; no `z-index` value was required.

Checked every other overlay reachable from `/dashboard` for the same two failure modes: `FloatingCoachPanel` (via `FloatingCoachLauncher`), `WearableWelcomeModal`, and `FirstCheckinTransition` are all rendered as siblings at the page's root level (not nested inside the hero's scrim/content wrappers), so neither bug ever applied to them — confirmed by measuring their bounding boxes (all viewport-relative, no negative offsets) and screenshotting each fully open. No other overlay/dropdown/modal/bottom sheet on this page was affected.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; targeted `eslint` clean; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing on a clean run (one interleaved run hit the same 2 known-flaky real-DB tests, unrelated, green on immediate rerun); `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright) at exactly 375×667: profile menu bounding box now `x:0, y:200.5, width:375, height:466.5` (full viewport width, floor-anchored) with all 6 items + header + Sign Out visible and painting above the bottom nav/floating chat button in the screenshot; re-checked in both the scored-hero and compact (zero-check-in) hero states. Zero console errors. Not deployed as of this entry.

**2026-07-25 fourth follow-up — "What Root Is Noticing" converted to an image-card carousel:**

`WhatWereNoticingCard`, `RootMapCard`, `CoachingMessageCard` ("From Root"), and `RecommendationsCard` went from four stacked white cards inside a tinted panel to a horizontal scroll of four ~3:4 image-backed vertical cards (`public/images/card-{noticing,rootmap,fromroot,habit}.jpg`), matching the rest of the redesign's image-card language.

- **New shared components**: `components/dashboard/NoticingTile.tsx` (the image tile shell — full-bleed photo, bottom gradient, gold kicker + cream headline; either a `Link` for a card with an existing destination, or a button that opens `components/dashboard/NoticingSheet.tsx`, a bottom sheet reusing the same mechanics as `ProfileSheet.tsx`) and `lib/dashboard/toHeadline.ts` (derives a ≤6-word headline as a literal in-order prefix of each card's own real sentence — never invented wording — falling back to the full sentence unchanged when it's already short).
- **No content lost**: Root Map and Recommendations tiles still navigate straight to `/root-map` and `/recommendations` exactly as their old "See your full Root Map"/"See all recommendations" links did — confirmed those destination pages still render their full real content. What We're Noticing and From Root had no dedicated destination page, so tapping them now opens a bottom sheet with every original section/list/link (including the dynamic `recommendedInvestigation` link) or the full `coachingCard` text (previously the "Tell me more" inline-expand target) — same data, same actions, just behind a tap instead of always visible. `CoachingMessageCardBody.tsx` (the old inline-expand toggle) is now unused and was deleted.
- **Rhythm check**: this zone's carousel could collide with the "Your Path" zone right before it, since that zone's last element is conditional — if `ComprehensiveAssessmentCard` (white) is absent, "Your Path" used to end on `MovementAssessmentCard`'s image-backed treatment, which would then sit directly against the new image-card carousel. Fixed by reordering "Your Path" to start with `MovementAssessmentCard` (always renders) and end with `QuestionnairesHomeCard` (row) or `ComprehensiveAssessmentCard` (white) — both non-image treatments — so the boundary never clashes regardless of which cards are present that day. The "Trends" zone right after Noticing was already an unconditional white card, so no change was needed there.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on every touched/new file clean; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing; `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright) at exactly 375×667, logged in as `member.one@example.test`: all four tiles render with their real images (zero failed image requests), the row scrolls (confirmed via `scrollLeft`), text is legible against every image (one dark, one light cream, one dark, one gold — the bottom gradient covers all four), and every tap destination was exercised directly: the Root Map and Recommendations tiles navigated to their real pages (confirmed real page content, not an error page), and the What We're Noticing / From Root tiles opened their bottom sheets with the full original content visible above the hero and bottom nav. Zero console errors across all of it. Not deployed as of this entry.

**2026-07-26 fifth follow-up — fixed headlines, text/illustration overlap, gradient weight:**

- **Headlines**: replaced the `lib/dashboard/toHeadline.ts` word-truncation approach (which produced ellipsis-cut sentences) with four fixed, hand-written strings, one per card, passed directly as the `headline` prop in each of the four card components: "Four Doctors Assessment" (What We're Noticing), "An area worth exploring" (Your Root Map), "More context could help" (From Root), "Today's focus: Consistency" (Recommended For You). The full dynamic sentence each headline used to be derived from is unaffected — it's still exactly what each card's bottom sheet or destination page shows. `toHeadline.ts` had no other callers left afterward and was deleted.
- **Text/illustration overlap**: measured all four source images' actual illustration boundary (row-by-row pixel-variance analysis, not assumed) — three land right at 59.4% down, the fourth (`card-fromroot.jpg`) at 63.5%. `NoticingTile.tsx`'s tile grew from 172px to 196px wide (aspect-[3/4] kept, so height scales to ~261px) and its text block moved to `p-3.5` with a smaller `text-base`/`leading-tight` headline instead of `text-lg`/`leading-snug`. Measured the rendered text block's own top edge via Playwright afterward: it now sits at **74.4% down the card on all four tiles**, roughly 11 percentage points below the worst-case 63.5% illustration boundary — confirmed with real bounding-box numbers, not a screenshot guess.
- **Gradient weight**: the dark overlay used to be a full-height `bg-gradient-to-t from-black/90 via-black/15 to-transparent` covering the whole card. It's now confined to just the bottom 40% band (`absolute inset-x-0 bottom-0 h-[40%]`, `from-black/75 via-black/35 to-transparent`) — the illustrated 60% above it now has no darkening at all, and the band underneath is lighter at its lightest point than before while still keeping text legible on the lightest source image (`card-rootmap.jpg`'s cream background).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on every touched file clean; full `vitest run` — 1759/1759 passing on a clean run (one interleaved run hit the same known-flaky real-DB tests, unrelated, green on immediate rerun); `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright) at exactly 375×667, logged in as `member.one@example.test`: screenshotted all four cards individually (full-element screenshots, scrolling the carousel to bring each fully into view first) — every headline matches the requested text exactly with no ellipsis, the illustration is fully visible and undarkened on all four (dark green, cream, dark, gold backgrounds), and the text sits cleanly in the flat lower band on every one. ## Welcome flow entrance animation (2026-07-25)

Added sequenced entrance animation to the 4-screen member welcome/onboarding-intro flow. Confirmed this is the one live implementation first: `apps/consumer-web-app/app/page.tsx` (the post-login routing hub) redirects an eligible new member to `/welcome` (`apps/consumer-web-app/app/welcome/page.tsx` → `WelcomeFlow.tsx`) before the separate, later-stage `/onboarding` health-questionnaire flow; no duplicate/prototype copy of this screen exists elsewhere in the repo (per BUILD_STATUS's own "Two coexisting welcome/first-run flows" entry above — `/welcome` and `/onboarding` are two different features, not two implementations of the same one).

- **No new dependency**: the project has no JS animation library (`framer-motion`/`react-spring`/`gsap` all absent from `package.json`). Followed the existing hand-rolled CSS convention already used throughout `app/globals.css` (`.mef-animate-in`/`mef-fade-up`, stagger via inline `style={{ animationDelay }}`) rather than introducing one.
- **New CSS** (`apps/consumer-web-app/app/globals.css`): `.mef-fade-in` (opacity-only, for content that shouldn't also move — body copy, buttons) and `.mef-scale-settle` (the Screen 1 logo's "large to final size with a slight settle," done via a single `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)` overshoot easing curve rather than extra keyframe steps). Both are `!important`-disabled under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`, matching every other animation in the file.
- **`WelcomeFlow.tsx`**:
  - Screen 1 (Welcome to MEF Wellness): logo (`mef-scale-settle`, 0ms) → headline (`mef-animate-in` fade+rise, 150ms) → body copy (`mef-fade-in`, 300ms) → "Get Started" button (`mef-fade-in`, 450ms). Sequence completes at ~900ms.
  - Screen 2 (Your health is connected): the two paragraphs ("Many health concerns do not exist in isolation" / "MEF Wellness helps you understand...") were removed entirely, per the request — only the heading remains as static copy. The 4 health cards now stagger in 150ms apart (`mef-animate-in`) instead of appearing together; "Continue" fades in 150ms after the last card.
  - Screen 3 (What brought you here today?): heading and subtext fade in (`mef-fade-in`, 0ms/150ms), then the 13 goal tiles stagger in (`mef-animate-in`) starting at 300ms, 70ms apart, capped at `Math.min(index, 7)` so a long option list can't push the tail past the "under two seconds" budget regardless of list length.
  - Screen 4 (Let's Begin With Today) was untouched, as scoped — not mentioned in the request.
  - The step-wrapper `<div key={step} className="mef-animate-in ...">` (which replays a whole-screen fade+rise on every step change) now only applies `mef-animate-in` when `step === 4`; for steps 1-3 it's dropped so the new per-element sequences aren't double-animated underneath it.
  - `prefers-reduced-motion`: no explicit JS branching needed — the CSS classes' own animation, and nothing else, is what animates; disabling the keyframes at the media-query level (as done here) is sufficient for every element to render at its final, fully-visible state immediately, consistent with the rest of the codebase's reduced-motion handling.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx eslint` on the changed `.tsx` (exit 0; the one `no-html-link-for-pages` line is an unrelated pre-existing repo-root config warning, not an error); full `vitest run` — 165 files / 1759/1759 tests passing, including `tests/welcome.test.ts`'s 16 tests unaffected by this purely-visual change; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/welcome` route generated. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test` temporarily flagged `welcome_flow_eligible = true` for this test, reverted to `false` afterward) at exactly 375×667: logged in, landed on `/welcome`, and drove all 4 screens forward, confirmed "Back" from Screen 2 returns correctly to Screen 1 (state and animation both replay), then forward again through to Screen 4. Confirmed via DOM query that both removed paragraphs are gone (count 0) and the heading/4 cards/13 tiles/all buttons are present and correctly wired (a tile selection + "Continue" on Screen 3 correctly advances to Screen 4, which renders with "Start My First Check-In" present — not clicked, since it invokes a real server action). Zero browser console errors across the full run. Screenshots taken 1.5s after each screen mounted (past the ~1 second animation sequence) confirm every element is fully visible, correctly positioned, and matches the original screens' layout/spacing with no overlap or clipping. Not deployed as of this entry.

## Welcome flow rebuilt as a cinematic, auto-advancing intro (2026-07-26)

Replaced the 4-screen flow above with a 9-page sequence: 7 timed, auto-advancing "no button" cinematic pages, then the 2 existing interactive pages (goal selection, then the final screen) unchanged. Confirmed with the user up front that the final screen — not described in the new page-by-page spec, which stopped at goal selection — should be kept as Page 9, unchanged, rather than folded into Page 8's Continue button; this preserves the only code path that actually saves goals and marks `welcome_flow_completed_at`.

- **Page 1** (Logo + Welcome, alone): logo `mef-scale-settle` at 1200ms (slower than the entrance-sequence work above, via an inline `animationDuration` override on the same keyframe), then the headline (`mef-animate-in`) at an 800ms delay, then holds 3s, then auto-advances (~5000ms total, measured live at ~5.1-5.4s including timer overhead).
- **Page 2** (Story): a hand-rolled typewriter effect (`STORY_TEXT.length * 60ms` via `setInterval`, no library) reveals "Every person has a unique story." with a blinking caret (new `.mef-typewriter-caret` class, removed from the DOM once done), pauses 2s, then the paragraph fades+scales in together (new `.mef-scale-fade-in` keyframe — shared by this paragraph, Page 3's heading, and each Page 4-7 card, each overriding only `animationDuration` inline), holds 5s, auto-advances (~9.6s total).
- **Page 3** (Connected, alone): "Your health is connected" set in `uppercase` at display size, `mef-scale-fade-in` over 1.5s, holds 3s, auto-advances (~4.5s total). Nothing else on the page.
- **Pages 4-7**: the same 4 benefit cards from the old Screen 2, now one per page, centered, `mef-scale-fade-in` over 600ms, each holding 3s before auto-advancing (~3.6s each).
- **Page 8**: the goal-selection page, content and Continue-button behavior unchanged from the prior entrance-sequence work above. Does not auto-advance.
- **Page 9**: the prior "Let's Begin With Today" screen, unchanged. "Back" (only rendered here now — Pages 1-7 explicitly have no buttons, Page 8 "keeps its Continue button," singular) returns to Page 8.
- **Shared `CinematicPage` wrapper** (Pages 1-7 only): arms one `setTimeout` for the page's total duration and auto-advances on expiry; the whole page is a tap target that advances immediately (an `advancedRef` guard stops the timer and a tap from both firing); renders the required small "Skip" control (top-right, `stopPropagation`) that jumps straight to `GOAL_SELECTION_STEP` (Page 8).
- **Progress indicator**: the old 4-dot row was replaced with a single thin fill bar (`role="progressbar"`, width = `step / 9`) — better suited to a 9-page sequence.
- **Play-once persistence**: new column `profiles.welcome_intro_seen_at` (`supabase/migrations/00000000000102_welcome_intro_seen.sql`), same nullable-timestamptz/presence-is-the-signal pattern as `welcome_flow_completed_at` (migration 85) and `evening_reflection_reminder_shown_at` (migration 87) — a separate column from `welcome_flow_completed_at`, since the intro can be seen while goal selection/the final screen are still outstanding. New server action `markWelcomeIntroSeen()` fires once `step >= GOAL_SELECTION_STEP` (via full playback or Skip), idempotent so a returning visit's repeat call is a no-op. `app/welcome/page.tsx` now reads this column and passes `initialStep={GOAL_SELECTION_STEP}` instead of `1` when it's set.
- **Reduced motion**: a `useReducedMotion()` hook (one `matchMedia` check on mount, same convention as `RevealOnScroll.tsx`/`RootScoreCountUp.tsx`) is read once in `WelcomeFlow` and threaded down as a prop. When true: every page renders its final state immediately (typewriter shows full text at once, no caret); `CinematicPage` never arms the timer or tap-to-advance, and instead renders a visible "Continue" button. Skip remains available in this mode too.
- **Migration 102 applied to production**: confirmed via `supabase migration list --db-url ...` before (101 was the highest applied remotely, 102 pending) and after (102 now shows applied on both `db` and `local` sides) — applied with the user's explicit confirmation and the production database password supplied fresh for this one run, never written to a file.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on every touched file clean; full `vitest run` — 165 files / 1759/1759 passing, including the existing `tests/welcome.test.ts` eligibility suite (unaffected — it tests `isEligibleForWelcomeFlow`, not step content); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/welcome` route generated. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, resetting `welcome_flow_eligible`/`welcome_flow_completed_at`/`welcome_intro_seen_at` between scenarios, reverted afterward) at exactly 375×667, across 6 scenarios: (1) natural timing — Page 1 confirmed still showing at the 1.4s and 3.0s marks, auto-advanced at 5365ms measured against a ~5000ms design; (2) tap-to-advance — a tap during Page 1's animation advanced to Page 2 in 438ms; (3) Skip — visible on Page 3, jumped straight to Page 8; (4) Page 8→9 submission — tile selection + Continue reached Page 9 with "Start My First Check-In" present (not clicked), "Back" correctly returned to Page 8; (5) return visit — after Skip marked the intro seen, a fresh navigation to `/welcome` landed directly on Page 8, not Page 1; (6) reduced motion (Playwright `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`) — Page 1 showed a visible Continue button immediately, did not auto-advance after a 2.5s wait, and Page 2 showed the full sentence and paragraph at once with no typewriter delay. Zero browser console errors across all 6. Screenshots (with a settle wait added) confirm Pages 1-4 and 8 render fully and correctly, including the correct ~3.9s delay before Page 2's paragraph appears.

**Deployed**: pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `6523b6e`); Vercel auto-deployed a new Production build (`mef-platform` project, team `mef-wellness`) roughly a minute after the push, confirmed via `vercel inspect` — target `production`, status Ready, and `https://app.mefwellness.com` listed among its aliases, meaning the live domain now points at this build. Migration 102 was applied directly to the production database (see above). Not verified: any actual page content or timing in a browser against the live production URL — everything above was checked against the local dev server + local Supabase only, per the standing rule not to claim live-site verification that wasn't actually done.

## Home hero: time-of-day image switching (2026-07-26)

`components/dashboard/HomeHero.tsx`'s full-bleed hero photo now switches with time of day instead of always showing the same image.

- `public/images/home-hero.jpg` (the misty forest photo) was renamed to `public/images/home-hero-evening.jpg` (`git mv`, so history follows it); grepped first to confirm `HomeHero.tsx` was the only file referencing the old filename. A new `public/images/home-hero-day.jpg` (sunrise/coastline photo, supplied by the user) is the morning/afternoon image.
- New `heroImageForGreeting(greetingWord)` helper picks between them using the exact `greetingWord` value (`'Good morning' | 'Good afternoon' | 'Good evening'`) `HomeHero` already receives from `app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s `buildTimeContext()` (`lib/feed/timeContext.ts`'s `greetingForHour`: hour < 12 → morning, < 18 → afternoon, else → evening) — reusing the already-computed word rather than recomputing the hour independently, so the image can never disagree with the greeting text next to it. `'Good evening'` → the evening image; the other two → the day image, which is what "evening and night" in the request means here, since the existing greeting logic has no separate night bucket — anything from 18:00 through the next morning's <12:00 boundary is already all `'Good evening'`.
- `HeroChrome` (the shared chrome all three of `HomeHero`'s render branches — no-checkins, no-score-yet, full-score — call into) gained a required `heroImage` prop instead of a hardcoded `src`; all three call sites updated. The dark two-layer gradient, object-position framing, header chip styling, and all text treatment are byte-for-byte unchanged and shared by both images.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` clean; full `vitest run` — 165 files / 1759/1759 passing; `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually verified in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`) at exactly 375×667: since the greeting is computed from the member's real profile timezone against actual current time, verified both branches by temporarily setting the profile's timezone to one that places local time in the morning (`Asia/Karachi`, fixed UTC+5) and one that places it in the evening (`Pacific/Gambier`, fixed UTC-9) — reverted to `America/New_York` afterward. Confirmed via the rendered `<img>` `src` and the on-screen greeting text that `Good morning` paired with `home-hero-day.jpg` and `Good evening` paired with `home-hero-evening.jpg` in both cases — the two can't drift apart since one is derived from the other. Screenshotted both full-score hero states: header logo/"Rooted Reset" chip, avatar circle, greeting, Root Score, confidence label, explanation text, and "See your full Root Score" link are all clearly legible in cream/gold against both photos, matching the original single-image design exactly. Zero browser console errors in either state. Not deployed as of this entry.

## Welcome intro: back navigation + a more dramatic Page 1 (2026-07-26)

Two changes to `app/welcome/WelcomeFlow.tsx`'s cinematic intro (the 9-page sequence from the prior two entries above).

**Backward navigation (Pages 2-9):**
- `CinematicPage` (Pages 1-7's shared shell) gained an optional `onBack` prop. When present, it renders a small `ArrowLeft` icon button in the lower-left (`stopPropagation` on its own `pointerdown`/`click` so it can't also trigger the tap-to-advance handler beneath it) and enables swipe. Page 1 never receives `onBack` — it has no back control of any kind, per the request.
- Tap-to-advance was rebuilt on `pointerdown`/`pointerup` (replacing the old plain `onClick`) so the same gesture layer can also detect a swipe: a drag past `SWIPE_THRESHOLD_PX` (50px) that's more horizontal than vertical counts as a swipe — right advances backward, left advances forward; anything shorter is treated as an ordinary tap (forward).
- All backward navigation — the new arrow/swipe on Pages 2-7, and the existing "Back" text link — now goes through one shared `goBackOne()` (renamed from the old `goBack()`, which used to floor at `GOAL_SELECTION_STEP` since only Page 9 could go back before this). The "Back" text link is now also shown on Page 8 (previously Page 9 only), so going back from goal selection lands on the last benefit card page (Page 7).
- Replaying an animation on return needed no new machinery: the existing `key={step}` on the wrapper `<div>` in `WelcomeFlow`'s render already fully unmounts and remounts whichever page component is active on every step change, forward or backward — so a page's internal state (typewriter char count, `showParagraph`, the CSS animations themselves) always restarts from scratch. Going back from Page 8 to Page 7 "resumes the sequence normally" for the same reason — Page 7 remounts, replays, and its own auto-advance timer fires again on its own, carrying the member forward to Page 8 exactly as if they'd arrived there the first time.
- Reduced motion: the arrow/swipe layer is never attached (`onPointerDown`/`onPointerUp` are `undefined` when `reducedMotion`), but back navigation still works — a "Back" text link (matching the existing Page 8/9 link style) renders above the "Continue" button whenever `onBack` is provided, so reduced-motion users get the same manual-button pattern already used for forward navigation.

**Page 1: a slower, more dramatic title card:**
- New CSS in `app/globals.css`: `.mef-title-logo-in` (a 2s reveal using `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)`, a smooth deceleration curve with no bounce/overshoot — deliberately not `.mef-scale-settle`, which has a springy overshoot easing that would read as "snappy," the opposite of what was asked) scales the logo down from `2.4` with an animated gold `drop-shadow` glow that shrinks and fades out together with the scale, ending at scale `1` with no glow. `.mef-hero-bg-fade` is a solid `#0B1F16` (deep, near-black green) layer whose opacity animates from 1 to 0 on an 800ms transition delayed to start at 1.2s into the logo's 2s animation, so it stays fully opaque through the dramatic opening beat and only clears "as the logo settles."
- That background layer is rendered in `WelcomeFlow`'s own top-level JSX (`fixed inset-0`, `pointer-events-none`), not inside `PageLogoWelcome` — as a sibling of `<main>`, so it can cover the whole viewport (including the progress bar) rather than just Page 1's own content area. `<main>`'s container class (`CONTAINER`) gained `relative` so it paints above this fixed layer via the same "both positioned, DOM order decides" mechanism already documented in `HomeHero.tsx`'s hero chrome — no `z-index` needed or added.
- Timing: logo 2000ms, headline (`mef-animate-in`, unchanged fade+rise mechanism) starts once the logo/background settle completes and runs 1000ms, then a hold, then auto-advance. **Confirmed with the user first**: the literal per-phase numbers in the request (2s + 800ms + 1s + a full 3s hold) sum to ~6s, overshooting the request's own "roughly 4-5 seconds" total — the user chose to shorten the hold (to 2000ms, landing the total at exactly 5000ms) rather than compress the more specifically-numbered logo/headline phases.
- Reduced motion: the background overlay isn't rendered at all (not just animation-disabled) and the logo/headline skip their animation classes entirely, so a reduced-motion member sees the ordinary cream background and both elements at once, immediately — matching the same pattern used throughout the rest of the flow.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` clean; full `vitest run` — 165 files / 1759/1759 passing on a clean rerun (one interleaved run hit 2 known-flaky real-DB integration tests, unrelated to any file touched here, green on immediate rerun — consistent with prior entries' documented flakiness); `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, resetting welcome state between scenarios) at exactly 375×667:
1. **Page 1 sequence**: screenshotted at t≈0 (solid `rgb(11, 31, 22)` background, logo barely visible, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`), t≈900ms (logo larger with a visible warm gold glow), and t≈2300ms (background fully faded to cream, headline visible) — confirmed still showing Page 1 at t≈3800ms, then auto-advanced at 5730ms measured (close to the 5000ms design, within timer/render overhead).
2. **Back arrow**: confirmed absent on Page 1 (`button[aria-label="Back"]` count 0), present on Page 2; tapping it returned to Page 1 in 31ms and the background overlay was back at opacity 1 (screenshotted) — a genuine replay from the start, not a jump to the completed state.
3. **Swipe**: from Page 4, two left-swipes advanced to Pages 5 then 6 (confirmed via each page's exact benefit-card label), then a right-swipe correctly returned to Page 5.
4. **Back from goal selection**: the "Back" link (now present on Page 8) returned to Page 7 (confirmed via its label, "Receive personalized next steps"), which then auto-advanced back to Page 8 on its own without further interaction.
5. **Reduced motion**: Page 1 showed the cream background, logo, headline, and a Continue button all immediately (no `.mef-hero-bg-fade` element in the DOM at all); Page 2 showed both a "Back" text link and a "Continue" button with no visual collision; tapping Back correctly returned to Page 1.

Zero browser console errors across all 5 scenarios. Not deployed as of this entry.

## Camera capture-setup reproducibility gate (2026-07-26)

Body Assessment's standing-photo capture (front/left_side/right_side/back only — movement/video steps untouched) now blocks capture unless the camera's physical setup is reproducible, not just "the member is standing acceptably." Extends the existing `lib/body-assessment/cameraTilt.ts` and `poseValidation.ts` checks rather than adding a parallel system; measurement math, screening thresholds, results screens, and the coach review screen are all untouched.

- **Tightened tilt gate** (`lib/body-assessment/cameraTilt.ts`): roll (gamma) tolerance `±12°` → **`±1°`** of level; pitch (beta) tolerance `±40°` → **`±2°`** of vertical. Both now exported as `ROLL_TOLERANCE_DEGREES`/`PITCH_TOLERANCE_DEGREES` for reuse by the replication-matching logic below.
- **Tightened frame-fill gate** (`poseValidation.ts`): the existing `too_close`/`too_far` bodySpan check narrowed from a coarse `0.35–0.92` band to **`0.80–0.90`** ("subject fills 80-90% of frame height").
- **New hip-height gate** (`poseValidation.ts`): the old `camera_position` check (head+ankle-based `verticalCenter`, `0.38–0.62` band) replaced with a direct hip-landmark-midpoint check against a **middle-10% band (`0.45–0.55`)** — "camera at roughly hip height." Reordered to run after the posture-state checks (sitting/crouching/lying/lean) rather than before them, since a hip-height verdict on a non-standing subject isn't meaningful; existing tests updated for both the tightened bands and this reordering (see `tests/pose-validation.test.ts`, `tests/camera-tilt.test.ts`).
- **iOS motion-sensor permission**: `hooks/useDeviceTilt.ts`'s `requestDeviceTiltPermission()` now returns a typed outcome (`granted`/`denied`/`not_required`/`unavailable`) instead of firing silently; the request moved from a silent call on the Welcome screen's "Begin" tap to an explicit button + plain-language explanation on the existing `camera_positioning` prep screen (`AssessmentWizard.tsx`). The hook also exposes `hasReading` (true once a real `DeviceOrientationEvent` has actually arrived).
- **Manual bubble-level fallback** (new `components/body-assessment/ManualLevelBubble.tsx`): when permission was denied/unavailable, or no sensor event arrives within 2s of the camera step going live (covers "permission not required but no motion hardware" browsers), `CameraCapture.tsx` shows a static spirit-level graphic and requires an explicit "Confirm phone is level and steady" tap in place of the sensor check. Gating is otherwise identical (`tilt.ok` covers both paths).
- **Guided replication on re-assessment** (new `components/body-assessment/SetupReplicationPanel.tsx`, new `getMostRecentCaptureSetupAction`/`getMostRecentCaptureWithSetup` in `app/actions/body-assessment.ts`/`lib/body-assessment/data.ts`): before each standing-photo step, the member's most recent accepted capture of that exact view (across their entire history, not just this assessment — `capture_type` is reused across several `assessment_type`s) is fetched and shown side-by-side with the live reading (roll/pitch/hip position/frame fill). Capture is additionally blocked unless hip position and frame fill match the target within `±2%`/`±3%` (tighter than the absolute gate's own 10%-wide bands, since matching the *specific* prior setup — not just "somewhere in the broad acceptable range" — is what makes a before/after comparison valid). No target exists yet for a member's first-ever capture of a view, so nothing is blocked in that case.
- **Storage** (migration `00000000000103_body_assessment_capture_setup_reproducibility.sql`): five new nullable columns on `body_assessment_captures` — `roll_degrees`, `pitch_degrees`, `hip_mid_y_ratio`, `subject_frame_height_ratio`, `orientation_source` (`'sensor' | 'manual_fallback'`). `roll_degrees`/`pitch_degrees` are left `null` for a `manual_fallback` capture — there's no real sensor number to store, only the member's attestation. Applied to the **local** Supabase instance only (`supabase db push --local`) — **not yet applied to production**; needs the Settings→Database password to run against the linked project (see `reference_prod_supabase_migration.md`'s `--db-url` procedure) before this feature will work on the live site.
- **Explicitly not built here** (per the request): no changes to `postureMeasurements.ts`'s measurement math or clinical-screening thresholds, the member results screens, the coach review screen, or the existing spoken-guidance/message-priority pipeline (a tilt failure vs. a hip-height/frame-fill failure still surface in the same order as before this change; only the underlying bands were tightened).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both `consumer-web-app` and `shared-types-contracts` workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 29 warnings (matches the pre-existing baseline, none newly introduced); full `vitest run` — 165 files / **1763**/1763 passing (4 new tests added: 3 for the tightened frame-fill/hip-height bands, camera-tilt.test.ts's existing 8 rewritten in place for the new tolerances); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/assessment/new`. **Not verified**: this has not been driven in a real browser — `CameraCapture.tsx`'s effective tolerances (±1°/±2°) are extremely tight for a hand-held or casually-propped phone and may need a tripod/stand in practice; the manual-fallback timeout heuristic (2s with no sensor event) and the guided-replication side-by-side panel have not been exercised against a real device's DeviceOrientationEvent behavior. Not deployed; production database migration not applied — see click-by-click checklist delivered with this task for what to verify live.

## Two real-phone-testing bugs fixed in the reproducibility gate (2026-07-26)

**Bug 1 — contradictory framing instructions ("Step back" / "Move closer" alternating forever, no valid position).** Diagnosed (not just patched) in `lib/body-assessment/poseValidation.ts`'s top docblock: `not_full_body`'s hard clip bound (feet must stay above `ankleMid.y > 0.97`) and the previous `too_far` floor (`bodySpan >= 0.8`) were only simultaneously satisfiable when the hip-landmark midpoint sat near the exact center of its allowed `[0.45, 0.55]` band (added in the prior reproducibility-gate task). A member's hip position at the edge of that band (e.g. `hipMid.y = 0.55`, itself a passing reading) capped the max clip-free `bodySpan` at roughly 0.76 — below the old 0.8 floor, so no distance satisfied both checks at once, reproducing the exact reported alternation.
- **Fix**: `SUBJECT_FRAME_HEIGHT_MIN` widened `0.8` → **`0.65`** (max stays `0.9`), verified by the same calculation to leave real headroom at every hip position in the allowed band, including the worst-case edge (new test: `tests/pose-validation.test.ts`'s "passes a bodySpan of ~0.70 with the hip at the edge of its allowed band — the exact previously-impossible combination"). `not_full_body` (the hard requirement) is completely untouched — same 0.97/0.04 clip bounds as before, still checked first, never suppressed or smoothed.
- **New hysteresis** (`stepDistanceZone`, `poseValidation.ts`): a pure, independently-tested state machine — a `too_close`↔`too_far` reversal is held at the *previous* zone's message for up to 3s unless the opposite boundary is genuinely crossed by a real margin (`DISTANCE_DEADBAND = 0.05`). Deliberately scoped to ONLY the soft too_close/too_far pair — never applied to `not_full_body`, which stays immediate/hard per the request. Wired into `CameraCapture.tsx` via a new `distanceZoneStateRef`, feeding the same `continuityOverride` mechanism the existing multi-person/tracking-loss hysteresis already used, so the on-screen text (not just the raw per-frame status) is what's actually smoothed.
- **Stuck detector**: new `components/body-assessment/StuckGuidance.tsx` — after `STUCK_THRESHOLD_MS` (20s) with no successful capture on a standing-photo step, a large (`text-3xl`), full-frame, high-contrast overlay replaces the normal small on-screen cue with the single condition currently blocking capture. Ticks on its own 1s interval independent of the pose-detection loop.
- **Explicitly not changed**: the measurement math, finding thresholds, `cameraTilt.ts`'s roll/pitch tolerances, and the message-priority ordering for every OTHER check (multi-person, tilt, orientation, posture-state) — only the too_close/too_far pair got hysteresis, per the request's scope.

**Bug 2 — cannot exit the camera step.** `app/assessment/new/page.tsx`'s only "Back" control is a page-level `<Link>` above the wizard; once a full-bleed camera view is showing, it's easy to lose track of (and the camera screen itself, and the unsupported/denied error states, had zero exit control of their own).
- **Visible close control** (`CameraCapture.tsx`): a new always-rendered `X` button (top-left, `z-40` — above even the stuck-detector overlay, so it's reachable in every state) calling a new required `onExit` prop, present for every capture type (photo AND movement/video) and every phase, including the `unsupported`/`denied` error screens (which previously had no way out at all).
- **Camera release on exit**: a new `handleExit()` stops the `MediaRecorder` (if recording) and every `MediaStream` track *synchronously*, before calling `onExit()` — doesn't wait on React's unmount cleanup to eventually release the camera.
- **Navigation**: `AssessmentWizard.tsx`'s `onExit` routes to `/assessment` (same destination as the page-level Back link), so every way out of this screen is consistent.
- **Abandon-and-resume, no stuck record**: `AssessmentWizard.tsx` now checks, on mount, for a member's already-existing `'in_progress'` assessment of the same type (`getMyAssessmentsAction` + `getAssessmentDetailAction`, both pre-existing actions — no new server action needed) and resumes it — same `assessmentId`, already-captured steps skipped (re-fetching signed preview URLs via the existing `getSignedCaptureUrlAction`), landing on the first uncaptured step or Review if everything's already captured — rather than starting a brand-new row and orphaning the old one. A brief unrendered `checkingResume` beat avoids flashing the Welcome screen before jumping away from it. Known limitation: resuming skips the camera-positioning prep screen, so the explicit motion-sensor permission request isn't re-shown that session (falls back to the manual bubble-level path after its normal 2s grace window if the browser doesn't just resume delivering sensor events on its own).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 29 warnings (same pre-existing baseline); full `vitest run` — 165 files / **1771**/1771 passing (8 new tests: 7 for `stepDistanceZone`'s hysteresis state machine, 1 reconstructing the exact previously-impossible hip-edge/frame-fill combination); `npm run build` (run from `apps/consumer-web-app`, where the Next.js `build` script actually lives — the root `package.json` has no `build` script) — compiled successfully. **Not verified**: not driven in a real browser/phone — the original bug reports came from real-phone testing that this pass did not repeat; the stuck detector's 20s timer, the close button's reachability across every real device size, and the resume flow's actual DB round-trip have not been clicked through. Not deployed as of writing this entry (see the task's own report for push/deploy status).
