## Voice guidance regression from the bug-fix pass above, found and fixed (2026-07-26)

The previous entry's reproducibility-gate bug fixes silenced spoken guidance — reported from real-phone testing. Root cause diagnosed (not just patched):

- **The actual bug**: the main per-frame validation `useEffect` in `CameraCapture.tsx` had `tilt` (the whole object) in its dependency array. `tilt` is a fresh object literal computed at the top of every render (`evaluateCameraTilt(...)` or the manual-fallback branch), never memoized — so React saw it as "changed" after every render, including renders the effect's OWN `setValidation`/`setStableForMs` calls caused. This re-ran the effect in a tight loop, and `useGuidedVoice.speak()` always cancels whatever utterance is currently playing before starting a new one — so every cue was technically spoken (the code path ran) but never audible, since each call canceled the previous one before the browser could finish it. Fixed by depending on `tilt.ok`/`tilt.message` (stable primitives) instead of the object.
- **Caught the same class of bug while adding the new stuck-detector effect**: its dependency array initially included the whole `guidedVoice` object (also a fresh object every render — `useGuidedVoice` only memoizes its individual functions via `useCallback`, not the object it returns). Fixed to depend on `guidedVoice.speak` specifically before it ever shipped.
- **The 20s stuck message is now spoken**, not just displayed (`StuckGuidance.tsx`'s text) — a direct `guidedVoice.speak()` call on the rising edge of stuck mode, using whatever the main validation effect most recently determined was blocking (`latestBlockingMessageRef`), routed through the same `markSpeechStarted`/`markSpeechEnded` bookkeeping the rest of the guidance pipeline uses so the routine per-frame correction cadence can't cancel it mid-sentence.
- **Fixed a z-index bug that would have defeated the recovery path**: the stuck-mode overlay (`z-30`, full-frame) sat above the existing "Tap once to enable voice guidance" recovery banner (`z-20`) — once 20s passed with audio blocked, the recovery tap would have become permanently unreachable underneath it. Both the "blocked" and "unavailable" voice banners are now `z-50`, above `StuckGuidance`'s `z-30`, so the recovery tap stays reachable no matter how long the member has been stuck.
- **New explicit audio-unlock prep screen**: the `preparation` intro step (first screen after Welcome) now has a real, tap-triggered "Enable voice guidance" button — not just the existing silent `primeBrowserSpeechSynthesis()` priming — that speaks an actual audible confirmation line and shows live feedback from `guidedVoice.status` ("Voice guidance is working," or "We couldn't confirm audio played — check your volume/silent switch" if the mobile-autoplay unlock genuinely fails even from a real tap). Shares the same `'assessment-guidance'` id as `CameraCapture.tsx`'s own instance so the two coordinate through the existing `playbackRegistry` rather than talking over each other. Non-blocking — same "advisory, not required" posture as the existing motion-sensor permission screen, since on-screen text remains a working (if secondary) fallback for a member with no speaker.
- **Confirmed unaffected/working**: the countdown (`READY_PROMPT`/`TAKING_PHOTO_PROMPT`), the step-intro script, and every other correction cue all go through the same `guidedVoice.speak()` call the fixed effect now reaches at its normal (not runaway) cadence — no separate fix needed for those once the dependency bug was corrected.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 warnings (29 pre-existing + 1 new, same accepted "intentionally omit a stable-callback's parent object" pattern already present twice elsewhere in this exact file); full `vitest run` — 165 files / 1771/1771 passing (no test changes needed — this was a React-effect timing bug, not covered by the existing pure-function test suite). `npm run build` — compiled successfully. **Not verified**: not driven on a real phone — the regression itself was only caught by re-reading the diff and reasoning through React's effect-dependency semantics, not by hearing it fail or hearing it fixed. The prep screen's live `guidedVoice.status` feedback is a genuine functional check (a real gesture-triggered `speak()` call, not a simulation) but still needs to be exercised on an actual device to confirm the "blocked" branch's copy reads sensibly if it ever fires. Not deployed as of writing this entry.

## Member goals as first-class data: welcome-flow goals no longer a dead-end write, `primary_concern` no longer asked twice (2026-07-26)

Closed the gap this doc's own "Two coexisting welcome/first-run flows" entry flagged: the welcome flow's "What brought you here today?" screen wrote `profiles.welcome_flow_goals`/`_other` (migration 86) with zero consumers, and the onboarding questionnaire separately asked a differently-shaped `primary_concern` question cold, later, driving `lib/onboarding/adaptivePlan.ts`'s concern-bank engine. Two problems, one build: give the welcome-flow answer a real consumer, and make it the one the member never has to repeat.

- **New follow-up screen** (`app/welcome/WelcomeFlow.tsx`): after the existing multi-select goal screen (still Step/Page 8), a new Page 9 asks "Which one, if it changed, would matter most right now?" — single-select, options limited to only the goals she just picked. If she picked exactly one, this screen is skipped entirely and that goal is auto-promoted to primary (`goNext()`'s `goals.length === 1` branch); `goBackOne()` correctly skips back over the never-shown screen too. The flow is now 10 steps total (`TOTAL_STEPS`), Page 10 is the unchanged final "Let's Begin With Today" screen.
- **New history table** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000104_member_goal_selections.sql`): `member_goal_selections` — `member_id`, `goals` (jsonb, the full selected set), `primary_goal` (nullable), `goals_other` (free text, never normalized/rewritten), `source` (`'welcome_flow' | 'onboarding_confirmation'`), `created_at`. Insert-only by design — RLS grants member-own `select`/`insert` and deliberately no `update`/`delete` policy at all, so a changed primary goal is always a new row, never an edit; "current" is simply the most recent row (`lib/member-goals/data.ts`'s `fetchLatestMemberGoalSelection`). **Backfill**: one row per profile with a non-null `welcome_flow_goals`, carrying `goals`/`goals_other`/`welcome_flow_completed_at` over as-is, `primary_goal` left `null` — the follow-up question didn't exist yet for those members, so nothing is guessed.
- **Vocabulary bridge** (`lib/welcome/goals.ts`'s new `WELCOME_GOAL_TO_PRIMARY_CONCERN`): the welcome flow's 13-key goal vocabulary and onboarding's 12-value `primary_concern` enum (migration 68) aren't 1:1 — two welcome goals (`strength_fitness`, `sports_golf_performance`) collapse onto `'performance'`, and two others (`understand_my_body`, `work_with_coach`) onto `'general_optimization'`, since neither pair has a closer dedicated concern. This is the only place the translation happens; `lib/onboarding/adaptivePlan.ts` never sees a welcome-goal key, only the mapped concern value, so its selection logic, the 9 concern banks, and `lib/onboarding/branching.ts`'s reassessment reorder are all completely untouched, per the request's constraint. `tests/welcome.test.ts` asserts every mapped value is a real key in `adaptivePlan.ts`'s own `CONCERN_CONFIG` (imported directly, not duplicated), so the two modules can't silently drift apart.
- **Onboarding confirms instead of asking cold** (`app/onboarding/page.tsx` → `OnboardingFlow.tsx` → `OnboardingForm.tsx`, member mode only): `page.tsx` fetches the member's latest `member_goal_selections` row; if `primary_goal` is set, it's passed down as `knownPrimaryGoal`. `QuestionField` (shared by every onboarding question, member/guest/reassessment alike) gained two new optional props — `promptOverride` and `renderOverride` — both `undefined` for every other call site, so no other question's rendering changed at all. When set, the `primary_concern` question renders `PrimaryConcernConfirmControl` instead of the cold enum picker: "Does that still feel right?" with a "Still true" button and a "Something changed" option limited to her other previously-selected goals (same "limited to what she picked" constraint as the welcome-flow follow-up). Whichever resolves calls `updateAnswer(PRIMARY_CONCERN_QUESTION_KEY, {status: 'answered', value: <mapped concern>})` — the exact same answer shape a real enum click would have produced — so `growAdaptiveSteps()`, `advanceAdaptivePlan()`, and the real `primary_concern` answer written to `onboarding_answers` on submit are all completely unchanged; only how that answer got chosen differs. A member with no known primary goal (never went through the welcome flow, or backfilled with none chosen) sees the exact original cold-ask question, byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Changing the answer** (`app/actions/welcome.ts`'s new `recordPrimaryGoalChange`): if she picks a different concern on the confirmation screen, a new `member_goal_selections` row is appended (`source: 'onboarding_confirmation'`, carrying `goals`/`goals_other` forward from her latest row) — never an edit to the welcome-flow row, so both stay queryable with their own timestamps, per the request's "never overwritten" requirement.
- **`completeWelcomeFlow`** (`app/actions/welcome.ts`) gained a third parameter, `primaryGoal`, validated against the submitted `goals` set; it still writes `profiles.welcome_flow_goals`/`_other`/`welcome_flow_completed_at` exactly as before (kept for backward compatibility / the existing eligibility check) and additionally inserts the first `member_goal_selections` row (`source: 'welcome_flow'`).
- **Explicitly untouched**, per the request: `lib/onboarding/adaptivePlan.ts`'s selection logic and concern banks, `lib/onboarding/branching.ts` (the reassessment flow's reorder-only path), the trend engine, the three-tier coaching language, and `member_pattern_states`.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx eslint` on every touched/new file — 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning in `OnboardingForm.tsx`, on a line this change didn't touch); full `vitest run` — 166 files / **1780**/1780 passing (9 new tests: 6 in `tests/member-goal-selections.test.ts` covering the table's RLS — member-own insert/read, cross-member insert/read both rejected, no update policy at all — and the "latest row wins" history behavior; 3 in `tests/welcome.test.ts` covering `WELCOME_GOAL_TO_PRIMARY_CONCERN`'s completeness against both `WELCOME_GOALS` and `adaptivePlan.ts`'s real `CONCERN_CONFIG`); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 64 routes generated including `/welcome` and `/onboarding`. All tests and the build ran against a local Supabase instance with migration 104 applied via `supabase db reset`. **Not verified**: not driven in a real browser — the new welcome-flow follow-up screen and the onboarding confirmation/change screen were reasoned through and typechecked but not clicked through in Playwright or by hand, unlike most other entries in this doc. See the task's own report for a click-by-click checklist.

**Deployed**: migration 104 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (the user supplied the Settings→Database password fresh for this run — never written to a file); confirmed via `supabase migration list --db-url ...` showing `00000000000104` applied on both the `local` and `db` (remote) columns afterward, and a `--dry-run` push beforehand confirming it was the only pending migration. Code pushed to `origin/main` — see the commit this entry ships with for the SHA; Vercel deploy status not independently checked beyond the push itself, per the standing rule not to claim live-site verification that wasn't actually done.

## Real cross-variable correlation engine — replaces the rule-based `crossAssessmentCorrelations.ts` (2026-07-26)

`lib/intelligence-engine/crossAssessmentCorrelations.ts` was named for what it should have done, not what it did: it checked whether both halves of five hand-written pairings had an active registry finding and printed a canned sentence — no statistical calculation anywhere in it. This replaces it with `lib/correlation-engine/`, which actually looks at a member's own daily numbers over time.

- **What it computes**: for one member, whether two of her tracked daily variables move together — real Spearman rank correlation (`lib/correlation-engine/stats.ts`), never Pearson, since every input is an ordinal scale (1-5 ratings, 0-5 pain, bucketed sleep-duration, binary flags).
- **Candidate pairs are data, not code** (`correlation_candidate_pairs`, migration 105): 18 pairs seeded from `MEF-driver-library-v1.md`'s driver list and goal-weighting table, restricted to the subset that maps onto a column that actually exists today (`daily_checkins` + `wearable_daily_metrics`) — most of the driver library (workstation load, footwear, caffeine/alcohol timing, meal regularity, training volume, posture-assessment findings) has no daily-loggable source anywhere in the schema yet, so those pairs are left out rather than faked. Two seeded pairs (`sleep_quality_stress`, `digestion_stress`) are the direct successors of two of the old file's five rules; the other three old rules paired a daily variable against a posture-assessment registry finding, which isn't a daily-paired time series and has no equivalent here — retired, not migrated. More pairs can be added with a plain `insert`, no deploy.
- **Goal-scoped, reusing last week's `member_goal_selections`** (`lib/correlation-engine/data.ts`'s `candidatePairsForGoals`): "an outcome she cares about paired with a driver weighted for that goal" is implemented by filtering candidate pairs to the member's own recorded goals (`fetchLatestMemberGoalSelection`, the table the welcome-flow-goals build above just made real) — a member with no goals on file gets every active pair instead of none.
- **Lag**: same-day and next-day only, tested independently, the stronger `|rho|` reported (`lib/correlation-engine/evidence.ts`'s `evaluatePair`).
- **Real evidence gate, one place, named constants**: `MIN_PAIRED_OBSERVATIONS = 21`, `MIN_SPAN_DAYS = 21` (both `evidence.ts`), `EFFECT_SIZE_FLOOR = 0.3` (Cohen's conventional "medium" correlation effect size — an effect-size floor, not a significance test), plus a variation guard (`stats.ts`'s `hasSufficientVariation`, `MAX_MODE_SHARE = 0.85` — no single value may be more than 85% of a series). A pair failing any of these never surfaces as a measurement, only as `insufficient_data`.
- **Split-window stability**: history is split in two by date; each half must independently clear the effect-size floor with the same direction as the full-history result, or `splitWindowAgreement` is `false` — a half with too little data to test at all (`MIN_SPLIT_WINDOW_OBSERVATIONS = 8`) counts as not-yet-provable, never as agreement by default.
- **Never interpolates**: `lib/correlation-engine/pairing.ts` pairs two variables only on the exact calendar date(s) a lag calls for — a missing check-in day breaks that one pair, nothing is carried forward or guessed.
- **Feeds the existing three-tier system, not a parallel one**: `member_pattern_states` (migration 93) widened to accept a fifth `signal_kind`, `'correlation_finding'` — the write path itself (`upsertMemberPatternState`) was already fully generic over `signal_kind`, so no change was needed there. `lib/correlation-engine/classify.ts` is a new, separate classifier (mirroring `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/signalState.ts`'s occurrence/tier structure and importing its `ESTABLISHED_MIN_SPAN_DAYS`-equivalent constant and `MIN_CONFIDENCE_TO_PERSIST`, but **not editing that file**, per the request) — a correlation is recomputed fresh from full history every scheduled run, so occurrence count grows via the same "same relationship reconfirmed on consecutive runs" mechanism `classifyCheckinMetricSignal` already uses, not a discrete event chain. `established_pattern`/tier 3 requires occurrence ≥ 3 **and** span ≥ 21 days **and** confidence ≥ 0.55 **and** split-window agreement, together — the same all-conditions-at-once structure `classifyRegistryFindingSignal` uses for its own established gate. `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/copy.ts` (the three-tier coaching language) is reused verbatim, unedited — it keys only on `state`/`tier`, never `signal_kind`.
- **This engine's own evidence record** (`member_correlation_findings`, migration 105): one row per (member, pair) — lag, direction, rho, observation count, span, split-window agreement, resulting tier, computed timestamp — upserted by `lib/correlation-engine/data.ts`. Separate from `member_pattern_states` on purpose: this is "what did we compute this run," the pattern-states row is "what the coaching layer sees" — the same split registry findings and check-in metrics already have between their own source data and the shared state ledger.
- **Recomputed on a schedule, never on page load**: new `app/api/cron/correlation-engine` route, staggered to `14:00 UTC` in `vercel.json` (after `wearable-daily` at 12:00 and `daily-coaching-scan` at 13:00, so it always runs against that day's already-synced wearable data) — same `CRON_SECRET` auth, service-role client, and per-member `Promise.allSettled` isolation pattern as `daily-coaching-scan`, copied deliberately rather than factored into a shared helper (matching that route's own self-contained precedent).
- **Old file retired, real dependent migrated, not silently broken**: `crossAssessmentCorrelations.ts` had exactly one real code consumer, `patterns.ts`'s `buildPatternInsights`. Deleting it outright would have silently zeroed out `rootCauseSignals.ts`'s `correlations` field (a real, coach-facing "Root Cause" composite this engine also builds, found by tracing every consumer — not just the immediate one). Instead: `MemberHealthProfile` gained a `correlationSignals` field, populated in `profile.ts` by reading the member's own persisted `member_pattern_states` rows filtered to `signal_kind: 'correlation_finding'` (via the existing, unmodified `listMemberPatternStates` reader — no new query needed); a new `lib/intelligence-engine/correlationPatterns.ts` turns the *earned* (tier 2+) subset into the same `PatternInsight` shape the old file produced. `patterns.ts` swapped one import for the other; `MemberIntelligencePanel.tsx` (coach UI) and every other presentation file are byte-for-byte untouched, per the request's "leave presentation alone" — they now render real, earned correlations instead of canned ones without any code change on their end.
- **Never claims causation**: `describeSignalForCoach`/`describeSignalForMember` (reused, unedited, from `copy.ts`) are the only place a correlation signal becomes a sentence, and neither ever says one thing causes another — this task's own scope explicitly stops at "produce the data and the tier," per the request; no member-facing correlation copy was written or changed.
- **Explicitly not changed**, per the request: `lib/intelligence/trendEngine.ts`, `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/signalState.ts`, the adaptive question bank, the reassessment flow, and the member Insights / coach Root Cause presentation layers (only the *data feeding into* the Root Cause composite view changed, at the `profile.ts` layer — no `.tsx` presentation file was touched).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `npx eslint` on every touched/new file — 0 errors, 0 new warnings (one pre-existing unrelated warning elsewhere in the build, in a file this task didn't touch); full `vitest run` — **172 files / 1860/1860 passing** (80 new tests across 7 new files: `correlation-engine-stats`, `-pairing`, `-evidence`, `-classify`, `-variables` (all pure-function), `correlation-engine-integration` (6 tests against real local Supabase — a genuine Spearman correlation computed from seeded `daily_checkins` history, occurrence/tier growth across two runs, goal-scoped pair filtering using a real `member_goal_selections` row, and RLS: member-own read allowed, cross-member read rejected, the reference catalog readable by any authenticated user), and `intelligence-engine-correlation-patterns` replacing the deleted rule-based test file); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 65 routes generated including the new `/api/cron/correlation-engine`. Local Supabase reset with migration 105 applied before every check above.

**Not verified**: not driven in a real browser (nothing user-facing changed — no UI screen exists for this data yet, per the request's own scope boundary); the cron route itself was never actually invoked end-to-end against a live Vercel deployment (only its constituent function, `runCorrelationEngineForMember`, was integration-tested directly); real wearable data (`wearable_daily_metrics`) — every `wearable.*` candidate variable — was not exercised against real provider-synced rows, since no wearable provider is configured in this environment (per `BUILD_STATUS.md`'s own "Not Built" section); those pairs will simply read as `insufficient_data` for every member until a wearable is actually connected, which is correct, expected behavior, not a bug.

**Deployed**: migration 105 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (the user supplied the Settings→Database password fresh for this run, reusing the same session's already-established procedure — never written to a file); a `--dry-run` push beforehand confirmed it was the only pending migration, and `supabase migration list --db-url ...` afterward confirmed `00000000000105` applied on both the `local` and `db` (remote) columns. Code pushed to `origin/main` — see the commit this entry ships with for the SHA. **Not verified**: whether `CRON_SECRET` and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are set in the actual Vercel production environment was not checked (only that the route requires them, matching the two existing crons exactly) — the new cron entry in `vercel.json` will not actually start firing on Vercel's schedule until the next deploy picks up that file; Vercel deploy status itself not independently checked beyond the push, per the standing rule not to claim live-site verification that wasn't actually done.

**2026-07-26 follow-up — a real test gap, found by being asked to show actual results, not by re-review.** Asked to show concrete pass/fail evidence for five specific synthetic scenarios (genuine relationship, non-relationship, lag-only relationship, holds-then-vanishes across a split window, heavy missing days), four already had real committed tests and were re-run to confirm: genuine (same-day, rho 1.0, split-window agrees), non-relationship (`null`, correctly refused), lag-only (correctly picked `next_day` at rho 1.0 over a weaker same-day reading), holds-then-vanishes (a real measurement at rho 0.56 that clears the base floor, but `splitWindowAgreement: false` correctly blocks it from ever reaching "established" — the base gate alone would have let this one through). The fifth, heavy missing days, had never actually been tested — the closest existing coverage (a single isolated gap in `pairing.test.ts`, and a too-little-data-overall case) didn't cover a long, heavily-sparse window. Constructed and ran it live in the conversation first (a seeded 70-day window with ~60% of days dropped, correctly found 26 real paired days, ignored one-sided partial logs, computed span from real dates), then converted it into a permanent, reproducible (fixed-seed) regression test in `correlation-engine-evidence.test.ts` rather than leaving it as a one-off console check.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on the changed file clean; full `vitest run` — 172 files / **1861**/1861 passing (the one new test). Not deployed separately — this is a test-only change with no schema or runtime behavior difference; pushed to `origin/main` in the same session as the migration 105 deploy above.

**2026-07-26 second follow-up — is EFFECT_SIZE_FLOOR set at a sensible height?** Every prior fixture in `correlation-engine-evidence.test.ts` was noiseless (rho exactly ±1, or a hand-verified ~0) — real enough to prove the mechanism works, but silent on whether the 0.3 floor is actually a reasonable line between "real" and "noise" for a scattered, realistic relationship. Two fixed-seed fixtures added (a seeded LCG generating `outcome = driver + bounded random noise`, clamped back into the 1-5 scale, calibrated by hand against `stats.ts`'s own ranking/correlation math until landing near a target rho, then frozen as literal arrays — never regenerated at test-run time): a genuinely noisy but real relationship, confirmed via the actual `evaluatePair()` at **rho 0.5050695154937643**, same-day, split-window stable — correctly detected and clears the floor; and a weak relationship at raw same-day rho ≈0.267 — confirmed via `evaluatePair()` to return **`null`** (it tries next-day too; neither lag clears 0.3) — correctly refused.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on the changed file clean; full `vitest run` — 172 files / **1863**/1863 passing (2 new tests). Test-only change, no schema/runtime difference; pushed to `origin/main`.

## Driver Library + adaptive daily check-in (2026-07-26)

Seeds `MEF-driver-library-v1.md` as editable database data, gives every driver a per-member `unknown/watching/ruled_out/implicated` state driven only by the correlation engine's own verdicts, and points the existing adaptive question picker (`lib/adaptive-assessment-engine/select.ts`, unmodified — the same engine onboarding uses) at the daily check-in. The correlation engine, trend engine, three-tier language, `member_pattern_states`, the onboarding questionnaire flow, and the reassessment flow are all untouched.

- **Schema** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000106_driver_library.sql`): `driver_domains` (7 rows, the library's section headers), `drivers` (35 rows, the full Part 1 list — id, domain, label, what it observes, wearable-observable/posture-derived flags), `driver_goal_weights` (the Part 2 High/Medium table only — "Low" is the absence of a row, per the source doc), `member_driver_states` (one row per member/driver, written only by the new driver-state engine), `driver_probe_questions` (the rotating-probe bank — editable data, no deploy needed to add a question), `daily_checkin_probe_answers` (local follow-up answers with no existing `daily_checkins` column), `member_daily_probe_selections` (an audit/recency log of exactly what a given day's check-in included).
- **Driver state** (`lib/driver-state-engine/`): reads `member_correlation_findings` (migration 105) plus an independent "was there actually enough paired data" check built from that engine's own exported pure functions (`dataSufficiency.ts` — reuses `pairSameDay`/`pairNextDay`/`hasSufficientVariation`/the named thresholds, never edits them) so a driver is only marked `ruled_out` once it was genuinely tested and came back weak, never just because it hasn't been asked about much. `implicated` = any pair reached "Qualified pattern" (tier 3); `watching` = still building evidence or not yet enough data; `unknown` = no daily-loggable source exists for that driver yet (most of the library — workstation load, footwear, caffeine timing, meal regularity, etc. — has none, same limitation migration 105 already documented). Recomputed by a new `app/api/cron/driver-state-engine` route, staggered to 14:30 UTC (after the correlation engine's own 14:00 run).
- **The picker itself is not new** (`lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/`): builds an `AdaptiveQuestion[]` bank each day with `weight = goal weighting × days-since-last-asked × how uncertain the driver still is` (a real product of three factors, kept in the same small-float range as `lib/onboarding/concernBanks/*`'s own weights so the engine's fixed "within 1 point of the best score, random among the tier" behavior stays meaningful), then calls the **same unmodified** `selectBatch()` onboarding uses. A driver marked `ruled_out` is excluded from the bank entirely, not just zero-weighted.
- **Fixed core vs. rotating probes, protected**: `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS` (pain, energy, sleep quality, sleep duration, stress, mood) is a hardcoded constant never passed through the picker at all — `buildProbeBank()` additionally throws if any seeded probe question ever reuses one of those keys, so a future data-entry mistake fails loudly instead of silently. Bedtime/wake time were deliberately **excluded** from rotation even though the column exists: `lib/wellness/morningReadiness.ts`'s eligibility gate hard-requires them for a Morning Readiness / Daily Wellness Score to exist at all, so rotating them out would have silently zeroed an existing score — the same "protect the inputs a downstream system depends on" failure mode the task called out for the correlation engine, just for a different existing feature. Night waking, night sweats, and bowel status (morning) and digestion/movement (evening) are safe to rotate — confirmed those three are explicitly optional-but-encouraged in that same file's own comment. `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT = 4`, `MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS = 10` (6 fixed + 4 rotating), both single named constants.
- **Wearable-skip mechanism**: `wearableSupply.ts` excludes a probe from the bank entirely when a connected wearable already supplies its metric code for the day — real and tested, but no seeded probe question sets a `wearable_metric_code` today (none of the five have a direct wearable equivalent yet), so it's dormant until a future probe uses it.
- **Local follow-ups, not a branching tree**: pain reported at 3+ (0-5 scale) reveals "Where is it, mainly?"; answering that reveals "What tends to make it worse?" — plain conditional UI state in `CheckinForm.tsx`, not routed through the generic picker's `requires`/`excludes` engine. Answers save to `daily_checkin_probe_answers` (no dedicated column exists for them).
- **A day's plan is stable, not re-rolled**: computed once and persisted to `member_daily_probe_selections`; a repeat visit the same day reconstructs the same plan instead of calling `selectBatch` again.
- **Wired into two existing surfaces**, both already-live pre-existing screens, no new page: `app/checkin/CheckinForm.tsx` (night waking/night sweats/bowel status now conditionally rendered) and `app/checkin/evening/EveningReflectionForm.tsx` (digestion/movement now conditionally rendered) — every other field on both forms (habits, notes, concern flag, the five Evening Reflection–only questions, morning soreness) is untouched.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `eslint` on every new/touched file clean (only the pre-existing, unrelated `no-html-link-for-pages` repo-root config warning, same as every prior entry); full `vitest run` — **178 files / 1908/1908 passing** (45 new tests: `daily-checkin-adaptive-{scoring,probeBank,plan}.test.ts`, `driver-library-weighting.test.ts`, `driver-state-engine-{classify,dataSufficiency}.test.ts` — including the required regression test that `buildProbeBank()` throws if a probe question ever reuses a fixed-core key, and an integration-style test against a hand-built fake `SupabaseClient` proving the fixed core survives even when every driver is ruled out); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 66 routes generated including the new `/api/cron/driver-state-engine`. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, migration 106 applied locally via `supabase db push --local` first): `/checkin` rendered all six fixed-core questions plus 4 of the 5 real rotating probes selected for that day; setting pain to "Significant" revealed the location follow-up, answering it revealed the aggravating-factor follow-up; `/checkin/evening` rendered its digestion/movement card conditionally; reloading `/checkin` showed the exact same rotating probes (plan stability confirmed); a full submit was queried directly against the local database afterward and confirmed real writes to `daily_checkins` (bedtime, night waking, night sweats, bowel status, pain), `daily_checkin_probe_answers` (pain location + aggravating factor), and `member_daily_probe_selections` (all 6 core keys + exactly the 4 rotating probes actually shown). Zero browser console errors across the whole run.

**Not verified**: the driver-state-engine cron route itself was never invoked end-to-end against a live Vercel deployment (only its constituent function was exercised indirectly through the picker's data reads, which for a brand-new member/table returns "no rows yet" rather than a computed state — that's correct, expected behavior for a member with no correlation history, not a bug); the wearable-skip path has no seeded probe to exercise it against real wearable data, per the reasons above; whether `CRON_SECRET`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are set for the new cron in the actual Vercel production environment was not checked (matches the existing three crons' own precedent).

**Deployed**: migration 106 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (the user supplied the Settings→Database password fresh for this run, never written to a file); a `--dry-run` push beforehand confirmed it was the only pending migration, and `supabase migration list --db-url ...` afterward confirmed `00000000000106` applied on both the `local` and `db` (remote) columns. Code pushed to `origin/main` (commit `35981a6`); confirmed via `vercel inspect` that a new Production deployment for the `mef-platform` project built and went Ready within a minute of the push, aliased to `app.mefwellness.com`, and that the live domain returns a normal 307-to-`/login` for an unauthenticated `/checkin` request (served by Vercel, not an error page). Not verified: the logged-in experience on the live production URL itself — only the local dev server was driven in a browser.

**2026-07-26 follow-up — closing a real gap in the "core is never excluded" test.** Asked directly whether an automated test would fail if a core metric were ever dropped, not just whether one existed. It didn't, genuinely: every assertion in `daily-checkin-adaptive-plan.test.ts` and `-probeBank.test.ts` compared a computed plan against `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS` itself — so an edit to that constant (e.g. accidentally removing `'checkin.mood'`) would have changed both sides of every comparison together and every test would have kept passing. Added a `REQUIRED_CORE_METRICS` list, hardcoded as six literal strings independent of the constant, plus four tests: the constant equals that literal list exactly; a freshly computed plan contains all six; a same-day-reconstructed plan contains all six; and a plan where every driver is ruled out still contains all six. Proved the fix actually catches the regression it targets before committing it — temporarily deleted `'checkin.mood'` from `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS`, confirmed all four new tests failed with a clear diff, then reverted and confirmed green again.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on the changed file clean; full `vitest run` — 178 files / **1912**/1912 passing (4 new tests; one unrelated file showed a stale-state failure on the first run — passed clean in isolation and on an immediate full-suite rerun, consistent with this suite's already-documented real-DB flakiness, not a regression). Test-only change, no schema/runtime difference; pushed to `origin/main`.

## Member Case View + Coach Case View (2026-07-26)

Presentation-only feature: a member-facing "case view" framed around her own stated reason for joining, reading exclusively from data other systems already produce — [[project_correlation_engine]]'s findings (via `member_pattern_states`, unedited write path), [[project_driver_library_adaptive_checkin]]'s per-driver states, and `member_goal_selections` (welcome flow). No correlation, trend, or driver-state logic was added — every classification decision was already made elsewhere; this only reads and formats it. The correlation engine, trend engine, three-tier rules, driver-state logic, and the check-in question flow are all untouched.

- **New library** (`lib/case-view/`): `header.ts` (titles the view from her primary goal, quoting free-text intake verbatim — never rewritten), `investigation.ts` (groups her goal-relevant drivers into flagged-likely-involved / currently-being-looked-at / ruled-out — ruled-out items stay visible, never dropped — plus a "not yet trackable" note for goal-relevant drivers with no daily-loggable source at all), `findings.ts` (reads `member_pattern_states` rows with `signal_kind: 'correlation_finding'` — the correlation engine's own existing output — and excludes `insufficient_data` even though its DB row nominally carries `tier: 1`, since it hasn't actually earned a tier), `overlay.ts` (builds the two-variable overlay chart's data from the correlation engine's own exported `extractDailySeries()`/`addDays()`, shifting the driver series forward a day when the finding's strongest lag is `next_day` so the two lines visually align on the pairing that was actually tested), `emptyState.ts` (progress-toward-evidence using the correlation engine's own `MIN_PAIRED_OBSERVATIONS`/`MIN_SPAN_DAYS` thresholds — never a fabricated finding), `goalProgress.ts` (the headline measure's cadence/prompt logic), `service.ts` (orchestration — computes nothing, only assembles).
- **One new table, deliberately outside the check-in flow** (`member_goal_progress_checkins`, migration 107): a plain periodic 1-5 self-rating of how the thing she came for is actually going, phrased in her own words (quoting the case header's title). Lives only on the case view page, prompted weekly (`GOAL_PROGRESS_RECHECK_DAYS = 7`) — not a `daily_checkins` column, not a `driver_probe_questions` row, never scored or classified. This is the view's headline measure, positioned above the driver detail per the request.
- **Tier-gated wording, not raw numbers**: member-facing sentences reuse `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/copy.ts`'s existing `describeSignalForMember`/`TIER_LABEL` verbatim, unedited — the same hedged, non-causal language every other signal in this app already uses. A finding only appears once it has reached `one_time_observation` or above; the overlay chart only appears at `repeated_signal` ("Repeated signal") or above, per the request.
- **Coach view** (`app/coach/clients/[id]/CaseViewPanel.tsx`): the exact same `CaseViewBody` component the member sees, with a `coachMode` flag that adds one thing a member never sees — a raw-numbers block per finding (observation count, span, Spearman ρ, split-window agreement, lag, direction, confidence %) — so a coach can see why something reached the tier it did. Same data, nothing computed twice. Access is enforced by RLS (`coach_read_assigned_*`), matching every other coach-facing action in this codebase — never an application-level assignment check.
- **Empty/early state treated as the primary screen, not a fallback**: when a member has zero earned findings (expected for most members' first three weeks), the view shows real check-in counts, real elapsed days, and — for each goal-relevant candidate pair — real observation/span progress against the same thresholds the correlation engine itself gates on. No generic wellness copy, no implied pattern.
- **No overlay/two-series chart existed anywhere in this codebase before this** (confirmed by research) — built in the same hand-rolled-SVG house style as every other chart here (2px lines, round caps, endpoint labels, one recessive hairline baseline, "View as list" fallback), with a genuine date-based x-axis (rather than the usual index-based spacing) specifically so a one-day lag offset is visually meaningful.
- **Entry point**: a plain link card on `/insights` (the closest existing "here's what we've learned" surface) — no bottom-nav change, no dashboard-carousel change, per this codebase's own explicit "three items, nothing more" design intent for the bottom nav.
- **A real bug found and fixed via actual browser verification, not typechecking**: React server-renders `<title>` (and a few other elements) expecting a single string child — `<title>{a} — {b}</title>`'s multiple interpolated children rendered as a genuinely empty string during SSR while hydrating correctly client-side, a real hydration mismatch that only showed up once the page was actually driven in a browser. Fixed in both new chart components by pre-joining each tooltip into one template-literal string before rendering it as `<title>{templateLiteral}</title>`. Confirmed the fix by diffing raw SSR HTML (fetched directly with the member's session cookie, bypassing the client entirely) before and after — before: `<title></title>`; after: `<title>Jul 26 — Better</title>`.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `eslint` clean on every new/touched file (only the pre-existing, unrelated `no-html-link-for-pages` config warning); full `vitest run` — **184 files / 1947/1947 passing** (35 new tests: `case-view-{header,investigation,findings,overlay,emptyState,goalProgress}.test.ts`, covering verbatim-quote preservation, ruled-out-items-stay-visible, the `insufficient_data`-despite-`tier:1` exclusion, the lag-offset shift, and the real-thresholds-not-fabricated empty-state math); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated including the new `/case`. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, migration 107 applied locally first): logged in as `member.one@example.test`, confirmed `/case` renders the case header ("Reduce pain or discomfort," from a goal selection inserted for this test), the goal-progress chart, and the empty-state ("3 check-ins so far, over 2 days... Pain and perceived stress load: 3 of 21 days logged") with zero console errors; submitted a goal-progress rating and confirmed it persisted; logged in as `coach.one@example.test` (seeded assignment to member.one) and confirmed `/coach/clients/[id]` renders the new Case View panel with raw-numbers language, zero console errors. The hydration bug above was caught and fixed during this pass — confirmed via a direct SSR HTML diff, not just the absence of a console warning.

**Not verified**: no member in the local seed data has an earned (tier ≥ 1) correlation finding yet (21+ days of paired history is the base gate), so the findings list, the overlay chart, and the tier-gated member/coach sentence rendering were exercised only via unit tests against realistic fixtures, not against a real finding in a running browser — the empty-state path (the one requirement 6 calls "the most important screen") was the one actually driven live, since it's what every real account currently shows.

## Case View: entry points added, real goal-selection backfill gap found and fixed (2026-07-26)

Follow-up to the Case View above — it had no way to be reached, and the site owner's own member account had no recorded goal for it to frame itself around. Both diagnosed and fixed. The case view's own content and layout were not touched, per the request's own scope boundary.

- **The Case View's URL**: `/case` (`https://app.mefwellness.com/case`). No route change — it was reachable by typing the URL directly the whole time, just not linked from anywhere a member would actually find it.
- **Confirmed the `/insights` entry point from the prior session was real and already in the code** (`app/insights/page.tsx`) — the report of "no link, page ends at the Energy Trend chart" describes `/progress` (which does have an Energy Trend chart and a "Coaching Insights" link near its bottom), not `/insights` itself; the two are adjacent, separate pages. Rather than leave this to interpretation, made the existing entry point impossible to miss regardless: it's now a full-bleed dark-green card with a gold icon (matching the site's own accent color), sitting immediately below the page's intro paragraph — above every insight card and above the trends section at the bottom. Confirmed via Playwright's bounding-box measurement that it renders at y≈213px, on-screen without scrolling on a 390×844 viewport.
- **Added a second, even more discoverable entry point**: `components/dashboard/QuickActionsCarousel.tsx` — "Your Case" is now the **first** pill chip in Home's Quick Actions row (before Movement/Food Lens/Progress), linking straight to `/case`. This is the same carousel every member already sees on Home without any extra navigation.
- **Checked the site owner's own production account** (`oakomah66@gmail.com`) directly against the production database: created 2026-07-14 — before the welcome flow existed at all (`welcome_flow_eligible: false`, `welcome_flow_goals: null`) — and zero rows in `member_goal_selections`. Migration 104's own backfill (2026-07-24) only covered profiles with a non-null `welcome_flow_goals` (members who'd gone through the *old* 4-screen welcome flow); an account that predates the welcome flow entirely was correctly outside that backfill's scope, but that left it with no goal at all for anything goal-scoped to read.
- **The real fix, not a one-off**: this account (and the platform's only other similarly-affected account) *did* answer a real "why are you here" question — onboarding's own `primary_concern` question, captured in their baseline `onboarding_submissions` row. Migration 108 reverse-maps that concern back onto `lib/welcome/goals.ts`'s `WELCOME_GOALS` vocabulary (the inverse of the existing `WELCOME_GOAL_TO_PRIMARY_CONCERN`) and inserts one `member_goal_selections` row per affected member — traceable back to her own answered question, never fabricated. Two concern values (`performance`, `general_optimization`) are ambiguous under the reverse mapping since two welcome-goal keys collapse onto each going forward; picked the more general reading in each case (`strength_fitness`, `understand_my_body`) rather than guessing at sport-specificity or routing anyone into the coaching-service-request goal she never selected. `healthy_aging` (a valid `primary_concern` value with no welcome-goal counterpart at all) is left unmapped — those members simply aren't backfilled rather than forcing a guess. Widened `member_goal_selections`'s `source` check constraint to add `'onboarding_backfill'` (additive, same pattern migration 105 used for `member_pattern_states.signal_kind`) and the corresponding `MemberGoalSource` TypeScript type.
- **Verified the actual blast radius before writing the migration, not after**: queried production directly and confirmed exactly 2 accounts platform-wide had a baseline `primary_concern` answer but no `member_goal_selections` row (`sleep` and `pain` — both unambiguous). Proved the migration's own INSERT logic against a synthetic local fixture before ever running it against production: inserted a fake pre-welcome-flow onboarding submission (`primary_concern: 'pain'`) for a local test account, ran the exact same SQL the migration uses, and confirmed it produced `goals: ['reduce_pain'], primary_goal: 'reduce_pain', source: 'onboarding_backfill'` — then cleaned up the synthetic fixture.
- **Confirmed the specific scenarios requested**: a member with a stated goal and exactly one check-in renders `/case` correctly — header shows her goal, the goal-progress chart shows the "no ratings yet" prompt, and the empty state honestly reads "1 check-in so far, over 0 days... Pain and perceived stress load: 1 of 21 days logged" (real numbers, not a placeholder), zero console errors. A member with no stated goal at all sees the same page structure with a neutral "What you're working on" placeholder instead of a goal title, and broad (not empty) driver coverage in the investigation panel — no crash, no blank page.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `eslint` clean on every touched file; full `vitest run` — **184 files / 1947/1947 passing** (no test changes this pass — this was entry points + a data backfill, not new logic); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/case`, `/dashboard`, and `/insights` all generated with no errors. Manually driven in a real browser (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, migration 108 applied locally first): confirmed the "Your Case" chip is the first Quick Actions pill on `/dashboard` and navigates to `/case`; confirmed the dark-green entry card on `/insights` with a real bounding-box measurement; confirmed `/case` for a goal + one-check-in account and separately for a no-goal account, zero console errors in every case.

**Deployed**: migration 108 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (password supplied fresh by the user this session, never written to a file); a `--dry-run` push beforehand confirmed it was the only pending migration; confirmed afterward by querying `member_goal_selections where source = 'onboarding_backfill'` directly against production — exactly the 2 expected rows, including the site owner's own account now showing `primary_goal: 'sleep_better'`. Code pushed to `origin/main` — see this entry's commit for the SHA. Confirmed via `vercel ls`/`vercel inspect` that the new deployment for the `mef-platform` project (the project `app.mefwellness.com` is aliased to, per `.vercel/project.json`'s linked `projectId`) built successfully, target `production` (not Preview), status Ready, and the alias list includes `app.mefwellness.com`. ## Coaching Insights (`/insights`) — four display-bug fixes (2026-07-26)

Confirmed the task's own assumption first: all four were presentation faults, not engine faults. The pattern engine, trend engine, three-tier language (`lib/longitudinal-intelligence/copy.ts`), and correlation engine are all completely untouched — every fix is in how an already-correct classification gets composed into text or shown/hidden on the page.

- **Bug 1 — bare tier sentences with no subject** (`"We noticed this once."` / `"You mentioned this once."` / `"We don't have enough information yet to say much about this."`, each with no nameable subject): root cause was that `app/actions/longitudinalIntelligence.ts`'s `getMyLongitudinalPicture()` mapped straight to `describeSignalForMember(signal)` and discarded the rest of the signal — `copy.ts`'s sentence templates were never designed to carry a subject themselves, because Case View (`lib/case-view/findings.ts` → `components/case-view/FindingsList.tsx`) already renders the subject as a separate bold label alongside that same sentence; embedding a subject inside `copy.ts` itself would have duplicated it there. Fixed at a new layer instead: `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/picture.ts` (new file, not in the shared barrel, zero other importers) composes `{ subject, sentence }` pairs using `topicLabelForSignal` (`lib/root-coaching-engine/topicLabel.ts`, reused unmodified — already the exact tool for turning a bare `WellnessMetricKey` or a registry finding's own human label into a short subject) alongside the unmodified `describeSignalForMember`. When `topicLabelForSignal` falls back to its own generic `'this area'` placeholder (a signal with no real nameable subject), the item is dropped entirely rather than rendered — per the task's own instruction, an empty slot beats a sentence with a hole in it. `app/insights/page.tsx` now renders each line as `{subject} — {sentence}` with the subject bolded.
- **Bug 2 — "Your Next Best Step" names nothing and links nowhere**: the Root Router's `RootRouterOutcomeView` (`lib/investigation-engine/routerOutcome.ts`) already carries a populated `investigation: { displayName, route }` for exactly the two outcomes whose message claims "a specific area worth exploring" (`focused_investigation`, `reassessment`) — it was simply never read. `getMyLongitudinalPicture()` now returns `nextBestStep: { message, investigation } | null` via a new `nextBestStepView()` (also in `picture.ts`); the page links `investigation.displayName` to `investigation.route` (same `Link ... as Route` pattern `components/dashboard/WhatWereNoticingCard.tsx` already uses for the identical `RecommendedInvestigationView` shape). If one of those two outcomes ever had no investigation attached, the whole card is suppressed rather than shown with a dangling claim — defensive, since by construction in `routerOutcome.ts` this can't currently happen.
- **Bug 3 — the page contradicted itself** ("Nothing to share yet" sitting directly above a "Your Longitudinal Picture" section that then said things): `insights.length === 0` (the 5-generator Coaching Insights Engine) and the Longitudinal Picture (a completely independent generator over the same underlying signals) were rendered from two unrelated conditionals, so a member with zero Coaching Insights but a real longitudinal signal saw both. `app/insights/page.tsx` now computes `pictureHasContent` (true if any of the four groups or `nextBestStep` is non-empty) and only shows the "Nothing to share yet" card when **both** `insights.length === 0` and `!pictureHasContent` — i.e., only when there is truly nothing anywhere on the page to show.
- **Bug 4 — "1 check-in so far, over 9 days" read like a glitch**: this exact phrase does not exist anywhere on `/insights` — a full-codebase search found it only in `components/case-view/CaseEmptyState.tsx` (Case View, `/case`). Flagged the conflict against this task's own "don't touch Case View, it's out of scope" instruction; the user confirmed the scope note doesn't cover this specific phrasing bug and asked for it fixed in place. Fixed via a new pure `buildStillBuildingSentence()` (`lib/case-view/emptyState.ts`) that leads with elapsed time ("It's been 9 days since you started, and you've logged 1 check-in so far...") instead of the old `"{count} so far, over {days} days"` construction that visually resembled a broken template. Same real numbers, never invented or rounded — just reordered so a low count over a long span reads as an honest fact instead of a mismatch. `CaseEmptyState.tsx` calls this one function instead of inlining the string.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean; `eslint` on every touched/new file clean; full `vitest run` — **185 files / 1960/1960 passing** (13 new tests: `longitudinal-picture.test.ts` covering subject composition, the "no nameable subject → drop the line" rule, and the next-best-step naming/suppression logic; 5 new tests added to `case-view-emptyState.test.ts` for `buildStillBuildingSentence`, including the exact "1 check-in / 9 days" case from the bug report); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/insights` and `/case` both generated with no errors. No schema change, so no migration to apply.

**Not verified**: no actual browser was driven this session (no Playwright or other browser-automation tool was available) — every claim above is from typecheck/lint/tests/build only, not from watching the page render. See the checklist delivered with this task for what to check by hand.

## Driver Question Bank — 88 check-in questions, generic renderer (2026-07-26)

Expands the check-in's driver-probe bank ([[project_driver_library_adaptive_checkin]]) from 7 rows to 88, covering all 33 non-posture drivers in `MEF-driver-library-v1.md` with 2-4 questions each (draft reviewed and content-approved by the site owner in `docs/driver-question-bank-draft-v1.md`). Also closes a real gap found while scoping this: the 7 existing rotating questions were never actually rendered generically — each was individually hand-coded into `CheckinForm.tsx`/`EveningReflectionForm.tsx` (own `useState`, own JSX, own button labels), which would not have scaled to 88 and made "add a question without a deploy" only true at the data layer, not the screen. The correlation engine, driver-state engine, adaptive-picker scoring, and fixed core are all untouched.

- **Schema** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000109_driver_probe_question_bank.sql`): adds `screen` (`'morning' | 'evening'`) to `driver_probe_questions` — which check-in surface a question belongs on, previously an unwritten convention (each screen's component hard-coded which exact `question_key`s it recognized). Backfills the 7 existing rows to match their already-shipped behavior exactly (digestion/movement → evening, everything else stays morning-default). Wires the existing, previously-undriven-by-any-driver `morning_soreness` column to MOV-5 (pure backend evidence, zero UI change — that field is asked unconditionally regardless of this table). Inserts 80 new rows: 2-4 per driver, covering all Part 1 drivers except MEC-2/MEC-3 (camera-assessment-only, correctly excluded per the source doc's own instruction) plus its Part 4 "three goals that behave differently" driver, `SLP-1`/`STR-1`/`FUE-3` getting a smaller supplementary set since they already have dedicated daily coverage (fixed core, fixed core, and a live hydration counter respectively) rather than a duplicate question.
- **Generic renderer** (`components/checkin/DriverProbeField.tsx`): draws a question's buttons from its own `response_type`/`options` instead of a hand-built block per question — boolean → Yes/No pills, scale/count → numeric pills, single_select → pills reading `.label` off `{value,label}` options or falling back to a title-cased raw string for the 2 pre-existing rows that still store plain strings (`bowel_movement_status`, `pain_location`/`pain_aggravating_factor` — left as-is, not rewritten). Same exact Tailwind pill classes the two forms already used, copied verbatim.
- **Local follow-ups generalized** (`lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/localFollowUps.ts`): the pre-existing pain-location/pain-aggravating-factor pattern (a `driver_id: null` row, shown by hand-coded client-side logic, excluded from the server-side adaptive bank since a day's plan is computed before any answer exists) is now a reusable mechanism — any `driver_id: null` row's `requires` array is evaluated live against whatever's been entered so far in the current, in-progress check-in, via `ruleSatisfied` (newly exported from `lib/adaptive-assessment-engine/select.ts`, not re-implemented). A follow-up's stored answer is cleared if its trigger later becomes false again (e.g. an answer is changed back), matching the discipline the original pain follow-ups already applied by hand. The two original pain follow-ups were deliberately **not** migrated onto this mechanism (their gate is a fixed-core threshold, not a Rule-expressible condition) — left exactly as they were.
- **Both check-in forms rewired**, same visual position and behavior for existing fields: `CheckinForm.tsx`/`EveningReflectionForm.tsx` now receive the full `DriverProbeQuestion[]` (not just `question_key` strings) via `app/actions/dailyCheckinPlan.ts`'s new `getLocalFollowUpQuestionsAction` (every active local follow-up for a screen) and `getProbeAnswersForDateAction` (already existed, now actually wired in as `initialProbeAnswers` so re-opening an already-answered check-in shows prior generic answers instead of resetting them — a real, if minor, existing gap fixed as a side effect). The two pages (`app/checkin/page.tsx`, `.../evening/page.tsx`) filter the day's rotating-probe picks by `screen` before handing them to their form.
- **Selection mechanics unchanged on purpose**: `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT = 4`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS = 10` and the fixed core were not touched — a day's 4 rotating picks are still chosen from the *entire* active bank (now 88 rows instead of 7) and simply split across the two screens by their `screen` value; a given day can show 0-4 on either screen.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 warnings, none in any file this task touched (same pre-existing, unrelated baseline every prior entry has documented); full `vitest run` — **186 files / 1968/1968 passing** (8 new tests in `daily-checkin-adaptive-localFollowUps.test.ts`, covering eligibility before/after a trigger, an `in`-rule against a multi-value parent, a numeric-threshold rule, no-`requires` = always-eligible, and `excludes` overriding a satisfied `requires`); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 68 routes generated including `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening`. **Manually driven in a real browser** (Playwright, installed standalone into the scratchpad so the project's own `package.json` was not touched; local dev server + local Supabase with migration 109 applied locally first, `member.one@example.test`): `/checkin` rendered the fixed core plus a real rotating probe (`night_waking_count`) through the new generic renderer with zero console errors; `/checkin/evening` rendered three brand-new questions from this batch (`variety_this_week` MOV-4, `desk_hours_today` MEC-1, `days_of_irregular_schedule_this_week` CTX-3) with correct prompts and option labels, confirming the day's 4 rotating picks really do split across screens by the new `screen` column (1 morning + 3 evening = 4, matching `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`); the new `got_up_hourly` local follow-up was confirmed absent before answering `desk_hours_today`, present immediately after selecting "Over 6 hours," and a real submit was queried directly against the database afterward confirming both `checkin_probe.desk_hours_today: "over_6h"` and `checkin_probe.got_up_hourly: true` were written to `daily_checkin_probe_answers`. Zero console errors across the whole run.

**Not verified**: the other 79 new questions were not each individually clicked through in the browser (only the 4 that happened to be selected for this member's random daily plan were) — their correctness rests on the same generic renderer/eligibility code path just proven for those 4, plus the local-database row-count/referential-integrity checks (88 rows, zero orphaned `driver_id`s, zero duplicate keys, all 33 eligible drivers covered) run directly against both local and production Postgres; a coach-side question-editing screen (the site owner's stated next step) does not exist yet — editing today requires a direct database update, same as migration 106's original 7 rows always did.

**Deployed**: migration 109 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (the site owner supplied the Settings→Database password fresh for this run, never written to a file; the first attempt against the connection-pooler port 6543 failed with a `prepared statement already exists` error — a known pgbouncer-transaction-mode quirk, not a migration problem — retried successfully against port 5432); a `--dry-run` beforehand confirmed migration 109 was the only pending one, and `supabase migration list --db-url ...` afterward confirmed it applied on both `local` and `db` (remote) columns; a direct query against production confirmed 88 rows (19 morning / 69 evening), matching local exactly. ## Coach Question Bank — manage the 88 check-in questions without a deploy (2026-07-26)

Builds the coach-side screen the [[project_driver_question_bank]] entry above named as its own stated next step: add, edit, retire/restore, and read all 88 `driver_probe_questions` rows in one sitting. The correlation engine, driver-state engine, trend engine, three-tier language, member check-in forms (beyond reading the same table they already read), `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS`, and the onboarding/reassessment question banks are all untouched.

- **Schema** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000110_driver_probe_question_admin.sql`): adds coach insert/update RLS policies to `driver_probe_questions` (migration 106 had platform-admin-only write access; coaches could read but never edit) — deliberately **no coach delete policy**, so "never DELETE a row, retire only" is enforced at the RLS layer itself, not just in application code. New `driver_probe_question_revisions` table — one row per create/update/retire/restore, storing the full before/after row plus who and when, written under the acting coach's own RLS-scoped session (never a service-role client) so `changed_by` is always a real, RLS-verified `auth.uid()`. New `replaces_question_key` column links a "retire and replace" pair.
- **The lock rule, enforced in the data layer, not a DB trigger** (`lib/driver-probe-admin/data.ts`): before any update, the real answer count is checked (`daily_checkin_probe_answers` for `probe_answer`-storage rows, a live `count(column) not null` query on `daily_checkins` for the handful of `daily_checkins_column`-storage rows). If it's above zero, `response_type`/`options` are refused with a plain-language explanation; prompt/driver/screen stay editable regardless. "Retire and replace" is the escape hatch: retires the old row (its answers keep their exact original meaning) and inserts a brand-new row with a fresh, auto-generated `question_key`.
- **Protected core stays protected**: `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS` (mood/energy/stress/sleep quality/sleep duration/pain) was never a table row to begin with — this screen renders those six read-only from the same hardcoded constant the check-in forms already use, with no edit/retire controls at all. `createQuestion`'s generated key always carries a `checkin_probe.` prefix (`lib/driver-probe-admin/slug.ts`), which structurally cannot collide with a `checkin.*` fixed-core key — plus an explicit rejection check as a second line of defense. `buildProbeBank`'s own regression test (which throws if any seeded probe ever reuses a fixed-core key, from the driver-library build) was not touched or weakened.
- **The screen itself** (`app/coach/questions`, reached from a Program-Library-style card on `/coach`, not a new nav tab — matches this codebase's stated "reached from the coach dashboard" convention): a **reading surface first** — every question's full prompt text, its key, response type, morning/evening placement, active/retired state, real asked/answered counts, grouped by driver, filterable by driver/screen/status/"never asked" and sortable by driver or by least-asked — that's how the 76 never-touched questions actually get found. Retired questions live in their own de-emphasized, collapsed-by-default section rather than disappearing. Inline prompt editing saves on blur, no separate page. A live preview renders the real member-facing `components/checkin/DriverProbeField.tsx` (not a redrawn mock) in a modal, reachable from every row. Revision history is a collapsed-by-default list under each question showing exactly what changed, before/after, who, and when.
- **Verified the "no deploy" claim rather than assuming it**: a coach-created question is read back through the exact same, unmodified `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/data.ts` reader and fed through the exact same, unmodified `probeBank.ts`/`select.ts` picker in a real integration test — no admin-specific code path in that chain at all.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 warnings, none in any touched/new file (same pre-existing baseline every prior entry has documented — two real lint errors, an unused helper and an unescaped quote, were caught by a first `npm run build` pass and fixed before this); full `vitest run` — **188 files / 1979/1979 passing** (11 new: `driver-probe-admin-integration.test.ts`, 5 tests against real local Supabase covering exactly the five assertions this task called for — retiring leaves recorded answers intact, `response_type`/`options` are refused once a question has answers (while prompt stays editable), a protected core question can't be edited or retired, an inline edit writes a revision record with real before/after values and the acting coach's id, and a freshly created question is picked up by the unmodified picker; `driver-probe-admin-slug.test.ts`, 6 tests for question-key generation). A real bug was caught and fixed by writing the protected-core test before trusting the code: the FIXED_CORE check in `updateQuestion`/`retireAndReplaceQuestion` ran *after* the row-existence check, which is always null for a fixed-core key (it was never a row) — so the protected-core error message was unreachable dead code. Reordered so the FIXED_CORE check runs first in both functions. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 69 routes generated including `/coach/questions`.

**Manually driven in a real browser** (Playwright, installed standalone into the scratchpad, project's own `package.json` untouched; local dev server + local Supabase, migration 110 applied locally first, `coach.one@example.test`): `/coach/questions` rendered all 88 questions with zero console errors; the "Has been asked" filter correctly showed 0 (a fresh local reset has no real check-in history yet) while "Never asked"/`all` showed all 88, confirming the filter is real and not a no-op; an inline prompt edit saved and survived a full page reload; clicking Retire moved a question into the de-emphasized "Retired (N)" section, and Restore brought the total back to exactly 88 after another reload; the live preview modal opened the real `DriverProbeField` with working option buttons; creating a new question (auto-generated key shown and confirmed) appeared immediately and survived a reload, proving a real database write, not an optimistic-only illusion.

**Not verified**: "retire and replace" was exercised only by the integration test (against a question with a real recorded answer), not clicked through in the browser this session; the revision-history panel's *rendering* was not opened in the browser (its data layer and content were verified via the integration test's assertions on `listRevisions`, and the UI code path is the same `listRevisionsAction` call used everywhere else, but nobody watched the collapsed history panel actually expand on screen); whether granting a second, non-seeded real coach account this same access behaves identically was not checked — only `coach.one@example.test`.

**Deployed**: migration 110 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (reusing this same conversation's already-supplied Settings→Database password, never written to a file); a `--dry-run` beforehand confirmed migration 110 was the only pending one, and afterward both `--dry-run` (`Remote database is up to date`) and `supabase migration list` confirmed nothing is pending and 110 is applied on both `local` and `db` (remote); a direct query against production confirmed the row count is still 88 (this migration only added structure — the revisions table, RLS policies, `replaces_question_key` — no new question rows) and that `driver_probe_question_revisions` exists with exactly the RLS policies intended. Code pushed to `origin/main` (commit `8e0020f`); confirmed via `vercel ls`/`vercel inspect` that a new Production deployment for the `mef-platform` project (`dpl_6EMzyCsNf4ToqLsSju9aPRXcnPmV`) went Ready about a minute after the push, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` among others; confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com/coach/questions` returns a normal 307-to-`/login` for an unauthenticated request, served by Vercel (not an error page) — the route exists and is protected. Not verified: the logged-in coach experience on the live production URL itself — only the local dev server was driven in a browser, per the standing rule not to claim live-site verification that wasn't actually done.
