## Forecast & Calibration Loop (2026-07-26)

A new return-hook mechanic, independent of the correlation engine, driver-state engine, trend engine, and every existing check-in/evening-reflection question: she predicts tomorrow's energy at the end of Evening Reflection, and tomorrow's Morning Readiness check-in grades it, with Root going on record too once it has real history to work from. New tables/logic only — `lib/adaptive-assessment-engine/`, `lib/correlation-engine/`, `lib/driver-state-engine/`, `lib/longitudinal-intelligence/copy.ts`, the question bank, `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS`, onboarding, and reassessment are all untouched.

- **Schema** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000111_energy_forecasts.sql`): two tables, `energy_forecasts` (her forecast) and `root_energy_forecasts` (Root's), each with a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger that raises an exception if the prediction fields (`predicted_energy_level`, `forecast_date`, etc.) ever change, or if any update is attempted once `scored_at` is already set — a permanent record enforced by the database itself, not just application code. No delete policy exists for members on either table. Scoring (`actual_energy_level`, `gap`, `scored_at`) is the one allowed one-time write.
- **Her forecast** (`lib/energy-forecast/`): asked as the one new question at the end of Evening Reflection (`app/checkin/evening/EveningReflectionForm.tsx`), same 1-5 "Exhausted/Low/Moderate/Good/High" scale the morning check-in's own energy question already uses. Skipping it writes nothing — no default is ever substituted (`recordForecastsFromEveningReflection`, `insertForecastIfAbsent`). If a forecast already exists for tomorrow, the form shows it as a locked readback instead of a second, silently-ignored control (`getTomorrowsForecastPrediction`).
- **The anchoring rule, enforced structurally, not just visually**: the morning check-in page and form (`app/checkin/page.tsx`, `CheckinForm.tsx`) have zero import of anything from `lib/energy-forecast/` — there is no code path through which a forecast value could reach that screen before submission, regardless of how the JSX is later reorganized. A static-scan test (`tests/energy-forecast-anchoring.test.ts`, same pattern as `tests/assessments-isolation.test.ts`'s existing guard) reads both files' real source and fails if either ever references the forecast module, table, or field names.
- **Root's forecast** (`lib/energy-forecast/rootForecast.ts`): a rolling mean of her real energy_level history (up to the last 7 real values), rounded to the nearest whole point, clamped 1-5. `ROOT_FORECAST_MIN_HISTORY_DAYS = 3` — chosen because a mean of fewer than 3 real points is just an echo of the last day or two, not a genuine basis, and because this loop's whole purpose is a return hook that works well before three weeks of history exists (unlike the correlation engine's `MIN_PAIRED_OBSERVATIONS`/`MIN_SPAN_DAYS = 21`, which this deliberately does not reuse — a next-day energy guess is a much smaller claim than a correlation). Below the minimum, Root writes no row at all — absence is the honest "not ready" signal, same discipline the correlation engine and driver-state engine already use for "no daily-loggable source yet."
- **Scoring** (`lib/energy-forecast/service.ts`): `buildEndingScreenView`, called once from the new `/checkin/result` page, scores whichever of her/Root's forecasts are outstanding for the day just submitted (idempotent — a revisit is a harmless no-op via `.is('scored_at', null)` plus the trigger as a second line of defense). Accuracy = within `ACCURACY_TOLERANCE = 1` point. `MIN_SCORED_FORECASTS_FOR_CALIBRATION = 5` — chosen because a percentage from fewer results swings too wildly (1/1 or 2/3) to mean anything, while still being reachable inside two weeks. The calibration section (percentages + chart) only renders once **both** her and Root's scored counts independently clear this bar — never a partial display of just one side.
- **The ending screen** (`app/checkin/result/page.tsx`) — its own route, same slot every day, not a panel on the check-in form. `CheckinForm.tsx`'s post-submit redirect changed from straight-to-`/dashboard` to `/checkin/result?date=...&firstCheckin=...` (the only touch to the existing check-in flow beyond the new evening question); the existing Milestone 4 first-check-in transition still fires from `/dashboard` afterward, just one hop later, via the forwarded `firstCheckin` param. Three states: no forecast yet (day one, or she skipped) shows a plain readback of that morning's answers plus a real check-in-count/days-elapsed sentence (reusing the correlation engine's own `MIN_SPAN_DAYS` for "patterns typically need about 21 days," never a re-invented number); a scored forecast shows what she predicted, what happened, and the gap in plain language; once she has an earned correlation finding (checked by directly reusing Case View's own unmodified `buildFindings` — never a re-derived decision that could disagree with `/case`), the calibration section is replaced by a link to `/case` instead of duplicating pattern-shaped content there. Root's own status (scored / not-ready-with-real-numbers / "didn't get a chance to predict") always renders alongside hers.
- **Calibration chart** (`components/checkin/ForecastCalibrationChart.tsx`): new component in the same hand-rolled-SVG house style as `case-view/OverlayChart.tsx` (2px lines, round caps, endpoint labels, hairline baseline, "View as list" fallback) — a new shape (two accuracy-percentage lines, not two raw variable series) so it isn't a drop-in reuse of the existing overlay chart, but follows its visual conventions. Has its own defensive `MIN_CHART_POINTS = 3` floor on top of the service-layer gate, so it can never render an empty frame or a one/two-point line even if a future caller forgets to check first.
- **A judgment call worth flagging**: the prompt's Part 4 doesn't say explicitly whether the day's forecast-grading readback should keep showing once she has earned findings, or whether the whole ending screen hands off to `/case`. Read it as: the single-day predicted/actual/gap fact is not itself pattern-shaped (it's not a claim about a relationship), so it keeps showing every day regardless of case maturity; only the *calibration-over-time* content (which is pattern-shaped — a claim about how her forecasting is trending) hands off to `/case` once earned findings exist, so it isn't duplicated in two places.

**Tests** (`tests/energy-forecast-{scoring,root,anchoring,integration}.test.ts`, 19 new): pure tests for the scoring math, the calibration threshold gate, and Root's minimum-history gate; a static-scan test for the anchoring rule; a real-local-Supabase integration suite covering the two immutability triggers (edit refused, forecast_date unchanged after the refusal, second scoring attempt refused, first score preserved), a skipped forecast writing zero rows (not a null-valued placeholder), and the calibration section staying hidden at 4 scored results and appearing at exactly 5 for both her and Root — proven against the real assembled `buildEndingScreenView`, not just the isolated threshold function.

**Every safety test proven red before being trusted green** — each protection was deliberately broken, the test confirmed to fail with a clear message, then reverted and confirmed green again: (1) added a fake import of the forecast module into `CheckinForm.tsx` — anchoring test failed, reverted; (2) commented out the `energy_forecasts` immutability trigger and ran `supabase db reset --local` — both the edit-refusal and double-score-refusal tests failed, reverted and reset again; (3) commented out the `root_energy_forecasts` trigger the same way — its edit-refusal test failed, reverted and reset again; (4) changed the skip-guard to substitute a default value (`predictedEnergyLevel ?? 3`) — the "no default" test failed with the fabricated row visible in the diff, reverted; (5) weakened `meetsCalibrationThreshold` and `computeRootForecast`'s minimum-history check — both pure tests failed, reverted.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 warnings, none in any new/touched file (same pre-existing baseline every prior entry has documented); full `vitest run` — **192 files / 1998/1998 passing** (one interleaved run showed 2 unrelated real-DB tests fail — `longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` and one other — both passed clean on an immediate rerun and in isolation, consistent with this suite's already-documented real-DB flakiness, not a regression from this work); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 72 routes generated including `/checkin/result`. A genuinely unrelated pre-existing test (`tests/server-actions.test.ts`'s "a coach can read their actively-assigned client's check-ins") started failing after this session's required `supabase db reset --local` cycles (needed to prove the immutability triggers) wiped ambient dev-database check-in rows that test silently depended on — no seed file actually creates one for `member.one`. Fixed by giving that describe block its own `beforeAll`/`afterAll` fixture instead of relying on leftover state from unrelated manual testing; the fix is test-only, no application behavior changed.

**Manually driven in a real browser** (Playwright, installed standalone into a scratchpad directory, project's own `package.json` untouched; local dev server + local Supabase, migration 111 applied locally first, `member.one@example.test`): confirmed the morning check-in page's rendered HTML contains no mention of "predict"/"forecast" anywhere; opened Evening Reflection, found the new "Predict tomorrow" section, selected "Good," saved — confirmed via a direct database query that a real `energy_forecasts` row was written for the real next calendar date with `predicted_energy_level: 4`, unscored; reloaded Evening Reflection and confirmed the question now renders as a locked readback ("Your prediction: Good") with no editable control remaining. Because scoring a real forecast requires an actual next calendar day to pass, the ending screen's other two states were verified by seeding two fixture day-pairs (via a service-role script, real Postgres rows, cleaned up afterward) at distinctive far-past dates and navigating directly to `/checkin/result?date=...`: the no-forecast state rendered the real readback (mood/energy/stress/sleep quality/pain), the real "1 check-in logged... 21 days" sentence, and Root's honest not-ready sentence with a real 0-of-3 count; the scored state rendered "You predicted Moderate, and today came in Good — 1 point higher than you expected" and "Root predicted Good — exactly right," and a direct query confirmed `buildEndingScreenView` had genuinely scored the previously-unscored fixture row as a side effect of loading the page. Zero browser console errors across the whole run. Screenshots taken of both states.

**Not verified**: the true day-two loop (predict tonight, get graded by the real next morning's check-in) was not observed across an actual calendar-day boundary — only proven piece-by-piece (real forecast-writing on submit, real scoring-on-load against seeded fixture data) since that requires either waiting a real day or manipulating the system clock, neither of which was done; the calibration chart itself (as opposed to its underlying data/gating logic, which is both unit- and integration-tested) was not visually exercised in a browser, since reaching 5 real scored days for both her and Root organically would take at least a week of real usage; the coach-facing read access to either new table (RLS policies exist and mirror an established pattern, but no coach-side UI reads them yet — this task didn't call for one).

**Deployed**: migration 111 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (password supplied fresh by the site owner this session, never written to a file); a `--dry-run` beforehand confirmed it was the only pending migration, and both a follow-up `--dry-run` (`Remote database is up to date`) and `supabase migration list --db-url ...` confirmed nothing is pending and 111 is applied on both `local` and `db` (remote). Code pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `9306f19`); confirmed via `vercel ls`/`vercel inspect` that a new Production deployment for the `mef-platform` project (`dpl_AzrYAZyq1TFR3PkHDEyaLfQ93PNu`, `.vercel/project.json`'s linked `projectId`) went Ready within about a minute of the push, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` among others. Confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com/checkin/result` returns a 307 to `/login?redirectedFrom=%2Fcheckin%2Fresult` (served by Vercel, route exists and is protected, not an error page). Not verified: the logged-in experience on the live production URL itself — only the local dev server was driven in a browser, per the standing rule not to claim live-site verification that wasn't actually done.

## Daily Check-In redesign — four-screen wizard, folded-in intro, data-driven question visuals (2026-07-26)

Replaces the single long scrolling form at `/checkin` (and `/checkin/evening`) with a screen-per-topic wizard, and folds the welcome flow's old closing screen into the check-in itself. The correlation engine, driver-state engine, `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS`, the six protected core questions, trend engine, three-tier language, `member_pattern_states`, onboarding/reassessment, and the coach-side Question Bank screen's own logic are all untouched — only how the check-in *presents* the same underlying data changed.

- **Structure**: `/checkin` is now 4 screens — Feeling (mood/energy/stress), Your night (sleep quality/duration, bedtime+wake, night wake-ups/sweats), Your body (soreness, pain, digestion), Anything else (habits, concern flag, notes) — via a new shared shell, `components/checkin/CheckinWizard.tsx` (progress dots, back affordance, staggered per-item entrance). `/checkin/evening` gets its own 3-screen grouping (How your day went / Your body / Anything else + forecast) rather than being forced onto the morning screen's 4, per the task's own "using its own section grouping" instruction. Rotating driver-probe questions and local follow-ups are routed onto the right screen by driver **domain**, not hardcoded per question key — `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/screenGrouping.ts` maps each domain prefix (`SLP`→night, `DIG`/`MOV`/`MEC`/`FUE`→body, `STR`→feeling(morning)/day(evening), `CTX`→other) and resolves a local follow-up's screen through its own parent question. Tapping an answer fills it, then auto-advances the screen ~250ms later (`hooks/useScreenAutoAdvance.ts`) once that screen's own required fields are complete — reopening an already-answered check-in seeds each hook against its *current* completeness so it never cascades through all 4 screens on load. Progress dots are also tap-navigable up to the furthest screen reached this visit, as an escape hatch in case an optional rotating question is skipped past before it's seen.
- **Question visuals are data-driven** (`lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/displayStyle.ts`, migration 112's nullable `display_style` column on `driver_probe_questions`): a question's rendering comes from its `responseType`, with `display_style` as an optional override — never a hardcoded per-question block. `DriverProbeField.tsx` picks among the new `components/checkin/scales/*` primitives (`BooleanPills`, `DotsCount`, `SegmentedControl`, `PillRow`) purely off that resolution, so all 88 existing rows (column unset) get a correct default and a coach-created question inherits one automatically. The four fixed-core questions named in the task (mood/energy/stress/sleep quality, which aren't schema rows) reuse the same visual-primitive pattern via purpose-built components — `FiveFacesScale` (gold→cream→green gradient, interpolated from the two locked brand colors, not new hues), `VerticalFillScale`, `TighteningShapeScale`, `FiveMoonsScale` — rather than duplicating the pill-button code those used to share. `single_select` renders as a horizontally-scrolling single row (`PillRow`) instead of ever wrapping, regardless of option count or label length.
- **Two purpose-built components**: `BedtimeWakeArc.tsx` — a circular 24-hour dial she drags to set bedtime/wake, showing the computed duration in the center, writing the exact same `actual_bedtime`/`actual_wake_time` "HH:MM" strings the two `<input type="time">` fields it replaces already did (no downstream change). `BodyOutlineTap.tsx` — a plain body-outline SVG with tappable hotspots writing to the existing `checkin_probe.pain_location` field; "widespread"/"other" (not real points on a body) stay as two plain pills below it. The severity scale itself is unchanged.
- **Chrome fixes**: the "Morning Readiness" section title (which duplicated the page title) is gone — its content is now under the "Your night"/"Your body" screen headings instead. The page intro's last two sentences are cut, first sentence kept, exactly as quoted in the task. "Actually logging for yesterday?" is now a small pill chip beside the title. `bowel_movement_status`'s `'none'` option now reads "Didn't go" instead of "None" (migration 112 converts that row's options to `{value,label}`, same mechanism every other question already used — the stored `'none'` value, and its history, is unchanged). `BottomNav` and `FloatingCoachLauncher` are no longer rendered on either check-in page at all while she's in the flow (previously always present) — as a side effect, the "Want to talk it through instead?" `RootQuickLink` card (which only worked by talking to that now-unmounted launcher) was removed from these two pages rather than left as a dead control; Root is still reachable from `/checkin/result` and everywhere else immediately after finishing.
- **Theming**: Morning's page background now runs a warm gold/cream gradient at the top fading to the app's usual base; Evening's runs the inverse (a subtle dark-green wash at top). Same locked palette (`#1B3A2D`/`#C4A050`/`#F5F0E4`) throughout; gold is never used as a fill, only as an accent (the wake handle on the bedtime arc, the warm end of the mood gradient). New `mef-checkin-stagger` keyframe in `globals.css` (400ms, 80ms stagger, via `StaggerItem`) degrades to a plain opacity fade under `prefers-reduced-motion`, reusing the existing `mef-fade-in` keyframe rather than a new one.
- **Intro page merge**: the welcome flow's old closing screen ("Let's Begin With Today", `PageFinal` in `WelcomeFlow.tsx`) is deleted — selecting a primary goal (or auto-promoting a lone selection) now finishes the flow directly (`TOTAL_STEPS` 10→9). Its copy now appears as two lines above the first question on `/checkin`'s Screen 1, gated on `isFirstCheckin` (already computed server-side from real check-in history), so it's shown exactly once, ever, at her actual first check-in rather than as a welcome-flow page she taps through before ever reaching one.
- **A gap noted, not invented around**: the task's Screen 3 spec named "soreness, pain, digestion" for the *morning* screen, but `digestion_rating` was deliberately moved to Evening Reflection in an earlier session ("more honest to ask once the day is done" — still true, left alone). Resolved via the existing `bowel_movement_status` question, which is already a morning, digestion-domain (`DIG-1`) question — it now renders under "Your body" alongside soreness and pain, satisfying the screen's named topics without contradicting or duplicating the earlier decision.
- **A trade-off worth flagging**: auto-advance only gates on each screen's own *required* fields (the ones that already validated at submit before this change), not on whichever optional rotating/local-follow-up questions happen to be selected for that day — so it's possible to be auto-advanced off a screen before an optional question below the required fields is seen. The tap-navigable progress dots are the mitigation (any earlier-reached screen stays reachable), but this wasn't tested against every possible daily rotating-question combination.

**Tests** (`tests/checkin-{display-style,screen-grouping,core-survives-four-screen-split}.test.ts`, `tests/welcome-flow-final-step-removed.test.ts`, 35 new): `resolveDisplayStyle`'s fallback-per-responseType and override-wins behavior; `screenGrouping.ts`'s domain routing for every domain prefix used by the 88-question bank plus an unknown-future-domain fallback, and local-follow-up parent-key routing; a static-scan (same pattern as `tests/energy-forecast-anchoring.test.ts`) confirming all six `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS` still have a live state setter wired into `CheckinForm.tsx` and are grouped correctly across Screens 1-3 (there's no component-rendering harness in this repo — `vitest.config.ts` runs a plain `node` environment, no jsdom/RTL, so this is a source-level guard, not a rendered-DOM one); a static-scan confirming the welcome flow's closing screen and its copy are gone and the check-in's own first-time intro is present. The pre-existing core-metric guard (`tests/daily-checkin-adaptive-plan.test.ts`) was run unmodified and still passes, since `constants.ts`/`plan.ts` were never touched.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces — 2 pre-existing coach-question-bank optimistic-update object literals needed a `displayStyle: null` field added once the type gained the field, in `QuestionBankPanel.tsx`/`QuestionRow.tsx`); `npx eslint` — 0 errors (30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file, matching every prior entry's documented baseline; 1 real error caught by a first lint pass — an unused `PAIN_LOCATION_OPTIONS` constant left over after replacing it with `BodyOutlineTap` — fixed before this); full `vitest run` — **196 files / 2033/2033 passing**; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated including `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening`.

**Not verified**: no browser-automation tool was available this session, so nothing above was watched actually rendering — the bedtime/wake drag arc, the body-outline tap targets, the auto-advance timing/feel, and the tap-navigable dots were reasoned through in code and covered by the static-scan/unit tests above, but never clicked through in a real browser. See the checklist delivered with this task for what to check by hand.

**Deployed**: migration 112 applied directly to the production Supabase project (`mef-wellness-production`, ref `piafgqstbibvllsnuike`) via `supabase db push --db-url ...` (password supplied fresh by the site owner this session, never written to a file); a `--dry-run` beforehand confirmed it was the only pending migration, and both a follow-up `--dry-run` (`Remote database is up to date`) and `supabase migration list --db-url ...` confirmed nothing is pending and 112 is applied on both `local` and `db` (remote).

## Daily Check-In redesign v2 — cinematic/section modes, real screen split, color-as-data (2026-07-26)

Supersedes the redesign above (which this task itself said to). The screen split from the first pass turned out to still be one screen with a dead progress bar in practice, and the earlier pill-based no-wrap rule was clipping real labels ("Below av…", "Overwhelmed…"). This pass replaces both, and adds a second density mode for a member's very first check-in. No schema change this time — every field this touches already existed. Correlation engine, driver-state engine, `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS`, trend engine, three-tier language, `member_pattern_states`, onboarding, and reassessment are all untouched.

- **Two real modes** (`lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/wizardUnits.ts`): a `CheckinUnit` is one focused thing to answer — a single question, or an already-combined gesture like the sleep arc. `groupUnitsIntoScreens` groups units by section for **section mode** (every check-in after the first — 3-4 related questions per screen, exactly what the old 4-screen/3-screen structure was meant to be) or gives every unit its own screen for **cinematic mode** (the member's very first check-in ever, `isFirstCheckin` — full ceremony, one question at a time). Neither mode ever splits an already-combined gesture (the sleep arc, the body-outline-plus-severity control) into smaller pieces. `hooks/useScreenAutoAdvance.ts` was generalized to a single hook watching whichever screen is current (a variable screen count, driven by the day's rotating-probe selection, rules out one static hook per screen) — reseeds its "was this already complete" baseline every time the screen index itself changes, so a pre-filled reopen or a backward/dot navigation never triggers a false auto-advance.
- **The real screen split** (`components/checkin/CheckinWizard.tsx`, rebuilt): one continuous progress track whose fill animates smoothly to each screen's position — never independent per-dot bars that each jump from 0 to 100 — with tick marks at each screen boundary, tappable up to the furthest screen reached this visit. Screen transitions are a real two-phase exit/enter (outgoing content fades up and out over 180ms, then incoming content fades in from below over 320ms via new `mef-screen-exit`/`mef-screen-enter` keyframes in `globals.css`) rather than a hard content swap.
- **Never truncate** (`components/checkin/scales/{ShortOptionRow,StackedOptionRows,shared}.tsx`): `MAX_INLINE_LABEL_LENGTH = 10` is the one place this threshold lives. `resolveSingleSelectLayout` (`DriverProbeField.tsx`, exported for direct testing) sends any option set with a label over that length to full-width stacked rows instead of a single-line segment; short sets get the single-row treatment. Applied uniformly to all 88 driver-probe questions via `DriverProbeField`, not audited/fixed one at a time. `SegmentedControl`/`PillRow` (the old truncating components) are deleted.
- **One unified selected-state language**: `TapBleedTile` (`scales/shared.tsx`) is the single control every generic question (booleans, counts, short/stacked option rows) now renders through — solid forest green fill, white label, subtle lift when selected; white card, soft border when not. The fill itself bleeds outward from wherever she actually tapped (a `clip-path: circle()` grown from pointer coordinates captured on pointerdown, new `.mef-bleed-fill`/`.mef-bleed-active` CSS) rather than swapping instantly; degrades to a plain opacity crossfade under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **Color as data, never good/bad** (`lib/checkin-color-ramps.ts`): mood (cool muted → warm gold), energy (pale sage → saturated green), stress/daytime-stress (open sage → deep clay, shared component — `CompressingRings`, concentric rings that compress toward the center as stress rises), and recovery (dim → luminous gold) each get their own two-stop ramp inside the brand palette — never a red/green axis, so no answer's "wanted direction" is legible from its color. Pain/soreness severity uses its own clay→terracotta ramp, explicitly never red. `TemperatureOverlay.tsx` layers a fixed-8%-opacity tint over the page (interpolating between a cool/dim and warm/bright anchor as `computeWarmth()` reads whichever hero scales are answered so far) — intensity never changes, only hue.
- **Screen 2, "Your night," is one combined object** (`components/checkin/SleepArc.tsx`, replacing the old bedtime/wake-only arc): drag to set bedtime and wake, duration shown in the center and derived into the *existing* `sleep_duration` bucket field (`deriveDurationBucket`, exported and unit-tested) — the separate "About how many hours did you sleep?" question is gone, the field it wrote to is not. A static conic-gradient sky (dusk → night → dawn → day) sits behind the dial. Night wake-ups are notches tapped directly onto the arc (only rendered on days the adaptive picker actually selected `night_waking_count`, so a rotating question doesn't quietly become permanent). Sleep quality (its own required, protected-core tap, `FiveMoonsScale`) also colors the arc's stroke — dim/muted at a low rating, a soft gold glow at a high one.
- **Screen 3, "Your body," combines soreness and pain** (`components/checkin/BodySeverityOutline.tsx`): one body-outline tap for location, then one severity gesture whose value writes to *both* `pain_discomfort_level` and `morning_soreness`. These two real columns have different pre-existing DB check constraints (`pain_discomfort_level between 0 and 5`, `morning_soreness between 1 and 5` — confirmed by reading migrations 13 and 63, not assumed) — rather than alter either constraint, the combined severity (0-5, pain's own range) is clamped to `max(severity, 1)` for `morning_soreness`, so a "None" (0) severity is honestly stored as soreness's own lowest valid value instead of violating a real, pre-existing constraint. Digestion (`bowel_movement_status`) is now three/four small icon tiles (`DigestionIconTiles.tsx`) instead of a pill row.
- **Screen 4 reaches a human**: `ConcernToCoach.tsx` shows the assigned coach's own initials avatar (`getMyCoachFirstName`, new server action reusing the existing `resolveAssignedCoach` lookup `lib/safety/data.ts` already had — degrades to "your coach" if none is assigned yet) and phrases the control as sending a note, not flagging a concern. "Nothing to add today" clears the optional notes field and gives a calm, confirmed state instead of an abandoned empty textarea.
- **Evening gets its own bespoke depth**, matching morning's rather than four identical white strips: `SunPathArc.tsx` (overall day rating, low point to a high arc), `CompressingRings` (daytime stress, the exact same component morning's stress screen uses), `EnergyPatternLines.tsx` (four small hand-drawn line shapes — steady/dipped/crashed/improved — that draw themselves in via `stroke-dashoffset` on selection), `RecoveryFill.tsx` (a vertical fill on its own dim→luminous ramp). Evening's own `isFirstCheckin` signal (new — checks `getRecentCheckins(1)` the same way morning does) means cinematic mode applies there too on the rare chance a member's very first check-in ever happens to be an evening one.
- **The ending** (`components/checkin/EndingMoment.tsx`): after a real, successful save, a brief screen blends the colors of whichever hero scales she actually answered into one soft radial glow ("Today, in one color… built from how you actually answered — nothing added") before continuing to the existing next destination (`/checkin/result`'s forecast/calibration screen for morning, `/dashboard` for evening — both completely unmodified, just reached one beat later). If nothing real exists yet, it says so plainly instead of inventing a color. No number or insight is shown here — only a color composed from her own literal answers.
- **Chrome fixes**: `CheckInModeSwitch.tsx`'s "Morning Readiness"/"Evening Reflection" pill no longer wraps at any supported width (`whitespace-nowrap`, each half sized to its own text instead of a forced equal split); the header block above both forms was tightened (smaller heading, tighter top padding) so the first question sits higher; the "Log yesterday" date chip and hidden bottom-nav/floating-chat-during-the-flow from the prior pass both carried forward unchanged.
- **A necessary compromise flagged, not hidden**: combining soreness and pain into one severity value (as literally instructed) meant reconciling two fields with different valid ranges — documented above and in `BodySeverityOutline.tsx`'s own comment, since "writing to both existing fields" and "don't invent schema" both had to be honored against a real constraint mismatch neither one anticipated.

**Tests** (`tests/checkin-{wizard-units,no-truncation,sleep-arc}.test.ts` new; `tests/checkin-core-survives-four-screen-split.test.ts` and `tests/welcome-flow-final-step-removed.test.ts` rewritten for the new architecture — old `key="screen-N"` markers no longer exist): `groupUnitsIntoScreens`/`isScreenComplete` for both modes (the task's own "write tests for the screen split" and "both modes"); `resolveSingleSelectLayout` against the exact real long labels named in the bug report plus several from the 88-question bank, and a boundary test at exactly 10 vs 11 characters (the "no-truncation rule"); `deriveDurationBucket` against all five buckets plus the literal 10:30pm→6:30am example (the "sleep duration still writing to the hours field" requirement); a static-scan confirming all six `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS` still have a live setter (pain's is now `setSeverity`, the combined gesture) and that mood/energy/stress share the "feeling" section while sleep quality/duration/bedtime-wake are one combined "night" unit. `tests/checkin-{display-style,screen-grouping}.test.ts` (from the prior pass) needed no changes — the display-style resolver and the domain-routing logic they cover are untouched by this pass. The pre-existing core-metric guard (`tests/daily-checkin-adaptive-plan.test.ts`) was run unmodified and still passes.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean, zero errors; `npx eslint` — 0 errors (30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file; 1 real error caught by a first lint pass, an unescaped apostrophe in `EndingMoment.tsx`, fixed before this); full `vitest run` — **199 files / 2052/2052 passing**; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated including `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening`. No migration this pass — nothing here needed a new column or table.

## Daily Check-In engine optimization — persistent Continue/Exit, one required check-in a day, 60-second ceiling, water/movement off the check-in (2026-07-26)

Fixes a real, previously-unverified navigation bug (auto-advance was the *only* path forward, and it structurally could never fire on a screen whose required fields were already satisfied on mount — every optional-only screen and every reopened already-answered screen), then rebalances the whole daily-question economy around "morning is the one required ritual." `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`/`MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS` were explicitly un-protected by this task (previous briefs' "do not change" is reversed here) — everything else named as protected (core-metric guard, correlation/driver-state/trend engines, three-tier language, `member_pattern_states`, onboarding, reassessment, the coach Question Bank screen and its live preview, data-driven rendering off `response_type`/`display_style`) is untouched.

- **Navigation bug found and fixed** (`components/checkin/CheckinWizard.tsx`): reading `useScreenAutoAdvance.ts` closely — it only fires on a *false→true* transition of a screen's own completeness. A screen with zero required units (Evening's old "body" screen, either form's "other"/anything-else screen) or a screen that's already complete on mount (reopening an in-progress check-in) never makes that transition, so `goNext()` was never called and the member was stuck with no way forward except the tap-navigable progress dots — which themselves only unlock as far as `furthestScreenIndex`, which also never advanced. `CheckinWizard` now owns a **persistent Continue button**, rendered on every screen (not just the last), sticky at the bottom of the viewport, disabled only while that screen's own required units are unanswered; on the last screen it becomes the real submit. Auto-advance still fires as a convenience layered on top, never the only mechanism. A distinct **exit control** (a plain outline-circle X, always present, top-left) sits beside — never overlapping — the existing tinted-pill back chevron; different shape, different fill, different position rule (X is always there, the chevron only appears once `screenIndex > 0`), satisfying "both exist, both visually distinguishable."
- **Exiting mid-check-in saves progress** (task's own "never discard answers"): both forms gained `saveProgressAndExit()`, wired to the new exit control — persists whatever's currently filled (nulls for the rest; the underlying schema already allows this) and returns to `/dashboard`. Two new, deliberately lightweight actions make this safe: `saveDailyCheckinDraft` (`app/actions/checkin.ts`) and `saveEveningReflectionDraft` (`app/actions/eveningReflection.ts`) share the exact same row-write path as the real submit (`insertCheckinRow`/`upsertEveningReflectionRow`) but skip every "this genuinely happened" side effect the real submit fires — no `morning_readiness_recorded`/`evening_reflection_recorded` event, no AI dispatch, no timeline entry, no Root Score recalculation, and (evening only) **no forecast commit** — her tomorrow's-energy prediction is enforced immutable once set (migration 111's own trigger), so committing it from a mid-exit draft could have permanently locked in a half-made guess. Safety screening of free-text notes is the one side effect kept on the draft path — never skipped anywhere else in this app either. A real, if minor, pre-existing gap was fixed as part of this: the pain-location/pain-aggravating-factor follow-up answers were never seeded from `initialProbeAnswers` on reopen (always started null) despite already being persisted — now seeded, so resuming after an exit genuinely never discards them.
- **One required check-in a day** (task requirement 2): `BottomNav.tsx`'s central Check-In button no longer time-swaps to Evening Reflection from 5pm onward (it used to, presenting the two as equally-weighted halves of one obligation) — always Morning now. `CheckInModeSwitch.tsx` is no longer a 50/50 segmented control: Morning renders as the solid primary pill, Evening as a lighter underlined link explicitly labeled "(optional)". The post-morning-submit `EveningReminderModal` ("Before your day ends, come back and complete your Evening Reflection") is deleted outright, along with its supporting plumbing (`markEveningReminderShown`, `profiles.evening_reflection_reminder_shown_at` reads) — a completed morning check-in now goes straight to the ending screen. No streak or missed-check-in logic was ever Evening-specific to begin with (confirmed by reading `lib/feed/streakIntelligence.ts`/`lib/ai/agents/accountability.ts` — a "streak" is any day with *a* check-in, morning or evening, never gated on both), so nothing there needed changing. Two new, low-key entry points reach Evening deliberately: a "Reflect now" link already existed on Dashboard's Daily Wellness panel (unchanged); a new plain "Evening Reflection · Optional · a short close to the day" row was added to `/today`.
- **CRITICAL gap found and closed — much larger than the task's own framing** (task requirement 2's "digestion and movement probes"): reading migration 109 directly, 69 of the 88-question bank (everything but the original 7 pre-88-expansion rows) defaulted to `screen = 'evening'`, spanning all of FUE/MOV/MEC/STR/CTX plus part of DIG — not just the two the task named. With Evening now optional and de-emphasized, leaving those 69 rows evening-only would have silently stranded roughly 15 drivers (not 2) without a live evidence source for any member who never opens Evening — the exact failure mode the task warned against, just far bigger in practice than its own example suggested. New migration 113 sets `screen = 'morning'` for every currently-evening row **except** `checkin_probe.movement_today`, which is deactivated instead (see next bullet) — bank composition is otherwise untouched, so this is a pure re-homing, not new content. `digestion_rating` (DIG-2) is now specially handled in `CheckinForm.tsx` exactly like the other four `daily_checkins_column`-storage probes (its own state, folded into `DailyCheckinInput`, mirrored into a merged answered-map so its local follow-up `digestive_symptom_type` still resolves eligibility correctly) — removed from `EveningReflectionForm.tsx` entirely, along with its now-empty "Your body" section (still valid in the type/section-order for a future evening-scoped question, but `groupUnitsIntoScreens` drops an empty section on its own).
- **Water and movement leave the check-in** (task requirement 4): water already had no check-in question (an earlier session's `HydrationTracker` had fully replaced it, dashboard-only) — it just gained a second home on `/today` (`components/checkin/HydrationTracker.tsx`, reused verbatim). Movement's `movement_today` self-rating *was* still a check-in question (Evening-only); it's now `MovementLevelTracker.tsx`, a new Today-screen quick-tap card, loggable any time. Both continue writing to the exact same `daily_checkins.water_cups`/`movement_today` columns every downstream reader (`lib/wellness/wellness-index.ts`, coaching-insights, the driver-state engine's MOV-2 evidence) already used — `logMovementLevel` (`app/actions/events.ts`) reuses the existing `submitEveningBodyCheckin` resubmission path (versioned-row-safe, preserves every other field including whatever the morning rotation already captured for digestion) rather than a new write path. `movement_today`'s `driver_probe_questions` row is deactivated (not folded to morning) — its evidence source is now the unconditional Today tap, arguably *more* reliable than a rotated-in check-in question ever was, satisfying "keeps a live evidence source" a different way than digestion's fold-back. Requirement 4's "must not be overwritten" holds structurally: `water_cups`/`movement_today` are always re-read/carried-forward live at submission time (`getTodaysHydrationTotal()`, `existingCheckin?.movement_today`), never a stale default.
- **60-second ceiling — the daily question budget is now follow-up-aware, not just a flat pick count** (task requirement 3): `ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT` 4→3, `MAX_DAILY_QUESTIONS` 10→9 (still `FIXED_CORE_QUESTION_KEYS.length + ROTATING_PROBE_TARGET_COUNT`, unchanged formula). The real change is *how* that budget is spent: `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/probeBank.ts`'s new `selectRotatingProbesWithBudget()` treats it as spendable units, not a pick count — a rotating probe with no known local follow-up costs 1 unit, one that *is* a follow-up parent (e.g. `digestion_rating`→`digestive_symptom_type`) costs 2, reserving room for the follow-up whether or not today's answer actually triggers it. A normal day spends the whole budget on 3 plain probes; a day where the picker happens to land on a follow-up-bearing question gets 1-2 rotating probes instead — "two or three," never more, by construction rather than luck. `plan.ts`'s `computeFreshPlan` calls this instead of the old flat `selectBatch`. One residual, explicitly documented exception: the **pain follow-up chain** (`pain_location`→`pain_aggravating_factor`, up to 2 extra questions) hangs off the fixed-core pain question, not a rotating pick, so it sits outside this budget exactly as it did before — on a high-pain day the true total can still exceed 9 by up to 2. Not engineered away; flagged in `constants.ts`'s own comment and in the tradeoff section below.
- **Bank coverage verified, not assumed**: `tests/checkin-bank-coverage-30day.test.ts` simulates 30 consecutive days of `getTodaysCheckinPlan` against a 90-question undifferentiated bank with a stateful fake `member_daily_probe_selections` table (so day 2's recency weighting genuinely reflects day 1's picks, not a fresh memoryless bank each time) — **≥75% of the bank was reached at least once over the 30-day window** at the new, lower daily count, confirming the reduced per-day rotation still cycles the whole bank rather than starving most of it. (81% observed on the seeded run this test pins.)

**Tradeoff, quantified honestly (task requirement 6)**: fewer questions per day is real evidence loss, not free. Before this pass: 4 rotating probes/day, drawn from an effectively ~19-morning/69-evening-if-opened split. After: 3 (sometimes 2) rotating probes/day, drawn from an 87-morning/1-deactivated pool. A driver needing, say, 3 independent daily observations to move from `unknown`/`watching` toward a correlation-engine verdict: previously, with ~19 morning-eligible questions competing for 4 daily slots and most drivers only reachable via Evening (itself gated on the member actually opening it), the realistic time-to-evidence for a morning-only member was often *worse* than the new number, not better — a driver whose only questions lived in the 69-evening pool could go weeks without ever being asked if she never opened Evening. Under the new scheme, every driver (except MOV-2, now covered by the unconditional Today tap instead) is morning-eligible, and the picker's recency weighting actively deprioritizes recently-asked drivers in favor of overdue ones — so despite fewer slots/day, the **realistic per-driver interval is flatter and more predictable** than before, precisely because the 15-driver "stranded behind Evening" problem is now closed. The genuine cost is at the margin: a driver that previously might get asked about twice in a good week (4 slots × favorable weighting) now more realistically gets asked once every 5-10 days depending on bank size and how many other drivers are still uncertain, which does lengthen the tail for drivers deep in a large, still-uncertain bank. This is a real slowdown for those cases, not a wash — surfaced here rather than shipped quietly, per the task's own instruction.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file (same documented baseline every prior entry has shown); full `vitest run` — **204 files / 2098/2098 passing** (46 new tests across `tests/checkin-navigation.test.ts` (Continue/Exit static-scan), `tests/checkin-draft-actions.test.ts` (draft-vs-real side-effect scan), `tests/checkin-optimization-integration.test.ts` (real local Supabase: `completion_seconds` round-trips through `submit_daily_checkin`, a Today movement tap survives a later check-in submission unclobbered and vice versa, the migration's `driver_probe_questions` screen/active outcome), `tests/daily-checkin-adaptive-budget.test.ts` (`selectRotatingProbesWithBudget`/`followUpParentKeys` unit tests), `tests/checkin-bank-coverage-30day.test.ts` (the 30-day simulation above)); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated including `/checkin`, `/checkin/evening`, and `/today`.

**Instrumentation added, not yet real data**: `daily_checkins.completion_seconds`/`evening_reflections.completion_seconds` (migration 113) are now populated by the client on every genuine final submission (`Date.now()` captured at first render, read at submit) — but this is a brand-new column on a brand-new deploy, so there is no real member completion-time data to report a median from yet. The task asked to "report the median time from any test data you generate" — the only data generated this session is the integration test's synthetic `completion_seconds: 47`, which is a fixture value, not a measured completion time, and reporting it as a median would be fabricating a number. The honest answer is: the instrumentation is live and correct (round-trip verified against a real local Postgres), and a real median becomes available once members actually use the redesigned flow.

**Not verified**: no browser-automation tool was available this session — the Continue button's actual disabled/enabled visual state, the exit control's tap target, the sticky-positioning behavior on a long screen (e.g. the habits list), the de-emphasized Evening link's appearance on `/today`, and the Today-screen `MovementLevelTracker`'s tap behavior were all reasoned through in code and covered by the static-scan/unit/integration tests above, but never watched rendering or actually tapped through in a real browser. A full morning check-in start-to-finish, a water log from Today, and the elapsed-time measurement the task specifically asked for were **not performed** — no browser access this session. See the checklist below for what to check by hand.

## Three Daily Check-In fixes — settled auto-advance (revealed follow-ups no longer skipped), 700ms glide-forward, sleep dial readability (2026-07-27)

Three independent fixes on top of the optimization pass above. No schema change (confirmed via `supabase db push --dry-run` before and after — "Remote database is up to date" both times). Protected list respected: the six core questions, correlation/driver-state/trend engines, three-tier language, `member_pattern_states`, onboarding, reassessment, the coach Question Bank screen and its live preview, data-driven rendering off `response_type`/`display_style`, and the 6-core-plus-up-to-3 daily budget are all untouched.

### 1. Auto-advance was skipping revealed follow-ups — root cause and fix
`hooks/useScreenAutoAdvance.ts`'s old rule was "fire once the screen's *required* units are all answered," full stop — it had no concept of the unit list itself, so an optional unit sitting on the same screen (revealed by an answer, or just already there) was invisible to it. Answering "How much stress are you carrying as you wake up?" with High made mood/energy/stress (the screen's only required units) complete, and the hook fired its fixed 250ms timer immediately — carrying her away before "What's most of today's stress coming from, if anything?" (an optional sibling on the same screen) could be seen or answered.

- **The fix**: the hook now takes the full screen (not a boolean) and computes a **settle signature** — every visible unit's key plus its answered state, joined into one string. A screen only "settles" once its required units are complete AND that signature hasn't changed for `pauseMs` (700ms) — so revealing a new unit, or answering an existing one (required or optional), re-arms a fresh 700ms window every time it happens. Only once nothing changes for a full 700ms does it advance. `isRequiredComplete`/`screenSettleSignature` are exported as pure functions specifically so this logic is unit-testable without a rendering harness (this repo's vitest.config.ts runs a plain `node` environment — no jsdom/RTL, so the hook itself, which uses `useEffect`/`useRef`, can't be executed directly in a test; the same constraint every prior check-in test in this codebase has documented).
- **Audit — every conditional follow-up in the bank had the identical fault, not just stress** (task's own instruction: "audit every conditional follow-up ... report which questions were affected"). Queried the live `driver_probe_questions` table directly rather than assuming from the migration files: **9 DB-driven local follow-ups** (`driver_id: null`, non-empty `requires`), all on `screen = 'morning'` post-migration-113 —
  - `checkin_probe.bedtime_later_than_wanted` → `checkin_probe.what_kept_you_up`
  - `checkin_probe.medication_or_supplement_change` → `checkin_probe.what_changed`
  - `checkin_probe.last_meal_timing` → `checkin_probe.late_eating_reason`
  - `checkin_probe.meals_skipped_today` → `checkin_probe.skipped_meal_which`
  - `checkin_probe.energy_crash_today` → `checkin_probe.crash_timing`
  - `checkin_probe.alcohol_present` → `checkin_probe.alcohol_drinks_count`
  - `checkin_probe.digestion_rating` → `checkin_probe.digestive_symptom_type`
  - `checkin_probe.desk_hours_today` → `checkin_probe.got_up_hourly`
  - `checkin_probe.emotional_load_today` → `checkin_probe.emotional_load_source`

  plus the **2 hardcoded pain follow-ups** (`pain_location` → `pain_aggravating_factor`, gated on the fixed-core pain threshold, not a DB `requires` row). All 11 chains share the exact same root cause and the exact same fix — none needed individual patching, since the fix lives in the shared hook every screen already calls.
- **A clarification on `stress_source_today` itself**: read directly, its `driver_probe_questions` row has `requires: []` — it is not conditionally gated in the schema at all; it's a plain rotating probe that (when picked for the day) already sits on the same "feeling" screen as mood/energy/stress from the moment that screen mounts, in section mode. The reported "reveals a follow-up" framing describes the *experience* (she never got a chance to see or answer it before being swept away), not a literal schema-level reveal — but the underlying defect and the fix are identical either way: the old hook was blind to any optional unit's presence, revealed or not. Reported here plainly rather than asserting a DB mechanism that doesn't exist.
- **Continue stays, verified independent**: `handleContinue` in both forms calls `goNext()`/`performSave()` directly and never references the auto-advance hook or its internal state — confirmed by a static scan (`tests/checkin-settled-auto-advance.test.ts`) that `handleContinue`'s own function body contains no reference to `useScreenAutoAdvance`/`advanceRef`. If settle-detection never fires for any reason, Continue works exactly as before.

### 2. Flow instead of tapping Continue
Already largely in place from the prior pass — the **glide transition** (outgoing content fades up and out over 180ms, then incoming fades in from below over 320ms, via `CheckinWizard.tsx`'s existing `mef-screen-exit`/`mef-screen-enter` phases) needed no changes; it was built in the v2 redesign and already matches "a glide, not a cut." The actual new work was the settle-then-pause timing described above — `useScreenAutoAdvance`'s default `pauseMs` is now 700 (was a flat 250ms tied only to required-completeness). The Continue button's own styling/behavior is untouched.

### 3. The sleep dial — three changes, no new schema
All three read/write the exact same `actual_bedtime`/`actual_wake_time`/`sleep_duration` fields the arc always has — confirmed via `supabase db push --dry-run` showing no pending migration both before and after this pass.

- **(a) Tap to open a native time picker**: tapping the printed "Bedtime 10:30 PM" or "Wake 6:30 AM" label now opens the phone's own time picker — a real `<input type="time">`, linked to the visible text via `<label htmlFor>` (not a custom modal), visually hidden via the standard `sr-only` pattern but fully focusable/tappable. Selecting a time calls the new `applyDirectTime()`, which writes through the *exact same* `onTimesChange(bedtime, wakeTime, durationBucket)` contract dragging already used — same fields, same derived duration bucket, verified by a source-scan comparing both code paths line for line (`tests/checkin-sleep-dial.test.ts`). Dragging the handles is untouched and still works.
- **(b) The dial is labeled**: the four quarters now read "12 AM" (top), "6 AM" (right), "12 PM" (bottom), "6 PM" (left) — matching the dial's own existing clockwise-from-midnight math exactly, so the labels are never at odds with where a time actually renders — plus 20 unlabeled minor tick marks at every other hour. The printed time and the arc itself now track a drag **live**: a new local `dragPreview` state (set synchronously in `updateFromPointer`, before the parent's own `onTimesChange` round-trip) means the display no longer waits on the parent's (potentially heavier) re-render to catch up, and clears on release, handing display back to the committed value.
- **(c) Pre-fill from her usual times**: a new pure module, `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/sleepMath.ts` (extracted from `SleepArc.tsx`'s own math, framework-agnostic, importable from a Server Component) plus `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/sleepHistory.ts`'s `typicalSleepTimes()`, computes a **circular median** (not a naive arithmetic mean, which would average a 23:30 and a 00:30 bedtime into nonsense noon) over her last 14 days of real `actual_bedtime`/`actual_wake_time` values — bedtime samples are rotated around a noon boundary specifically so a late-night wraparound doesn't break the average; wake samples don't need it. `app/checkin/page.tsx` computes this once per page load (reusing the existing `getRecentCheckins` call that already covered `isFirstCheckin`, widened from 1 day to 14 rather than adding a second query) and passes it to `CheckinForm`, which seeds `actualBedtime`/`actualWakeTime`/`sleepDuration` state from it **only when today's row has no bedtime/wake yet** (`existingCheckin?.field ?? typicalSleep.field ?? ''`) — a real answer is never overwritten. With zero history, `typicalSleep`'s fields are `null` and this falls through to the same plain `DEFAULT_BEDTIME_MINUTES`/`DEFAULT_WAKE_MINUTES` visual default the arc already showed (handles still render at 10:30pm/6:30am so she has something to look at), but — critically — that default is **never written into `actualBedtime`/`actualWakeTime` state**, so the "night" screen's required-completeness never quietly counts an invented default as her confirmed answer. Only a real history-based typical value pre-fills the actual submitted state.
- **A real, unrelated bug found and fixed while building (c)**: `parseTimeToMinutes`'s regex only matched plain `"HH:MM"`, but Postgres's `time` type (the actual column type for `actual_bedtime`/`actual_wake_time`) round-trips through supabase-js as `"HH:MM:SS"` — meaning a genuinely reopened check-in's real stored bedtime/wake was silently failing to parse and falling back to the hardcoded default the whole time, previously masked only by the default (22:30/06:30) happening to be a plausible-looking time. Fixed in `sleepMath.ts` by making the trailing `:SS` optional in the regex; pinned with a regression test.

**Tests** (`tests/checkin-settled-auto-advance.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-sleep-dial.test.ts`, both new; `daily-checkin-adaptive-plan.test.ts`/`checkin-core-survives-four-screen-split.test.ts` run unmodified and still pass): `isRequiredComplete`/`screenSettleSignature` pure-logic coverage including the literal stress scenario from the bug report and all 9 DB-driven follow-up chains plus the 2 hardcoded pain ones (the audit above, each proven to change the signature when revealed); a static scan proving Continue's independence from the auto-advance hook; `parseTimeToMinutes` accepting both `"HH:MM"` and `"HH:MM:SS"`; `typicalMinutesOfDay`'s circular median including the midnight-wraparound case; `typicalSleepTimes` end-to-end (a real 3-night sample, an empty-history all-null case, and a night with only one of the two times logged); a source-scan proving `applyDirectTime` (tap) and `updateFromPointer` (drag) write through the identical `onTimesChange(..., deriveDurationBucket(durationMinutes(...)))` shape; a source-scan for the dial's quarter labels/minor ticks and the live drag-preview state; a source-scan confirming `CheckinForm`'s bedtime/wake/duration state falls back through `existingCheckin` then `typicalSleep`, matching the "never overwrite a real answer" rule.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file (same documented baseline); full `vitest run` — **206 files / 2129/2129 passing** (31 new tests); `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated including `/checkin` (now 12.3kB, up from 11.8kB for the new dial UI).

**Not verified**: no browser-automation tool was available this session. The task specifically asked to "answer the stress question with High and confirm the follow-up is answerable before the screen moves; set a bedtime via the tap picker and confirm the arc and the printed time agree; drag a handle and confirm the time updates live" — **none of this was watched happening**. Everything above is verified via a real local Postgres (for the pure sleep-math functions, exercised directly, no DB needed there) and 31 new logic/source-scan tests, not by driving a browser. This is a real, meaningful gap for a task centered on *visual* readability (the sleep dial fix's whole premise is that the current dial is misread by sight) — the click-by-click checklist below is the only way to close it.

**Deployed**: no schema change, so no migration to apply — confirmed via `supabase db push --dry-run` (`Remote database is up to date`) both before and after this pass. Code pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`) — see this entry's commit for the SHA. Confirmed via `vercel ls`/`vercel inspect` that a new Production deployment for the `mef-platform` project went Ready, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com`. Not verified: the logged-in experience on the live production URL — no browser was driven this session.

Code pushed to `origin/main` — see this entry's commit for the SHA. ## Stability fix — auto-advance removed entirely, X and Back verified working, tested in a real browser for the first time (2026-07-27)

Every prior entry above for this feature carries the same disclaimer — "no browser-automation tool was available this session." That changed this pass: Playwright was installed in this repo (`apps/consumer-web-app`, devDependency) and used to actually drive `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening` end to end against the local dev server + local Supabase, logged in as the seeded `member.one@example.test` account, across two full runs plus a dedicated follow-up-reveal/submit run and an evening run — not reasoned through in code this time.

- **Auto-advance removed, not disabled**: `hooks/useScreenAutoAdvance.ts` (the settle-then-glide timer from the 2026-07-27 "Three Daily Check-In fixes" pass) is deleted outright, along with its own test file (`tests/checkin-settled-auto-advance.test.ts`). The one call site in each form (`useScreenAutoAdvance(currentScreen, clampedIndex, goNext)`) and the now-dead import are removed from `CheckinForm.tsx` and `EveningReflectionForm.tsx`. `CheckinWizard.tsx`'s own doc comment is updated to state plainly that Continue is now the only mechanism that ever advances a screen — no code in that file changed, since it already only ever wired `onContinue` to the button's own `onClick`, never called it itself. This is the fix for requirement 1: two systems (a timer and a button) racing to control the same transition is what the task named as the cause of screens jumping and controls going dead.
- **X and Back, investigated directly, not just read**: statically, both were already wired correctly — `onExit={saveProgressAndExit}` and `onBack={goBack}` in both forms, real distinct tap targets in `CheckinWizard.tsx` (a plain outline-circle X always present, a tinted solid chevron only once `screenIndex > 0`), no overlay or disabled state found sitting on top of either. Driven in a real headless Chromium session (touch-enabled, 390×844) against the actual dev server, both worked exactly as coded: X saved a draft and returned to `/dashboard`; Back returned one screen with every answer still selected. The most plausible real-world cause of "unresponsive," given the code already reads correctly: the now-removed 700ms auto-advance timer racing a manual tap — on a screen whose required fields are already satisfied on load (a reopened check-in, or a screen with no required fields at all), the old timer could fire and swap screen content out from under a tap in flight, landing the tap on whatever replaced it. Removing auto-advance entirely removes that race by construction, regardless of whether that exact mechanism was the one behind any specific report.
- **X and Back never overlap**: measured their bounding boxes via Playwright at 320/375/390/414/768px — both are fixed 36×36 circles in a flex row with the progress track taking the remaining space, never sharing horizontal space at any width tested.
- **Tests** (`tests/checkin-navigation.test.ts`, extended): the existing persistent-Continue/exit/back static-scan suite is kept, with the one assertion that used to require `useScreenAutoAdvance(` in each form flipped to require its total absence; new checks confirm the hook file no longer exists, that neither form's `useEffect` blocks (there are now none) call the screen-advancing functions, and that `CheckinWizard` never invokes `onContinue` itself (only wires it to the button). 19/19 passing.
- **What was actually driven in the browser** (not claimed, run): logged in as `member.one@example.test`; answered mood/energy/stress/any rotating "feeling" probes with 1-2.5s waits after each and confirmed the screen never moved; tapped Continue and confirmed it advanced exactly one screen; tapped Back and confirmed all three answers were still selected; measured X/Back overlap at 5 widths (none); reached screen 3 ("Your body"), tapped a body-location hotspot then a high severity (revealing the pain-aggravating-factor follow-up) with waits after each tap and confirmed the screen never moved at any point; tapped Back then Continue again and confirmed the severity answer survived; tapped X and confirmed landing on `/dashboard`; reopened `/checkin` fresh and confirmed the severity **and** the aggravating-factor follow-up were both restored; continued to the final screen and submitted, landing on the real `EndingMoment` success screen with zero console errors. Repeated steps 1/3/4/5 on `/checkin/evening`: first-question-doesn't-move, Continue advances one screen, Back preserves the day-rating and stress answers, X lands on `/dashboard` — zero console errors there either.
- **Test-DB hygiene note**: driving the flow against local Supabase wrote real rows (a synthetic prior-day check-in seeded to get the account out of first-ever-checkin/cinematic mode, plus whatever the flow itself saved on submit/draft/exit). All of it — `daily_checkins`, `evening_reflections`, `daily_checkin_probe_answers`, `member_pattern_states`, `daily_feed_items`, `member_daily_probe_selections` — was deleted for this account before finishing, and the full suite was re-run clean afterward. It briefly wasn't: an early raw-SQL seed row left in place caused two unrelated real-DB integration tests (`feed-integration.test.ts`, `longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts`) to fail on cross-contamination, not a code defect — caught and cleaned up before this pass finished.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file (same documented baseline every prior entry has shown); full `vitest run` — **204 files / 2123/2123 passing**; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening`. No migration this pass — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` showing no changes; nothing here touched schema.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/Chrome-on-iOS touch behavior specifically (this session's browser driving was headless Chromium with touch events simulated, not a physical device) — if the original report's root cause was a WebKit-specific quirk rather than the auto-advance race, it wouldn't necessarily reproduce or get caught here. The site owner's own click-through on a real phone is still the final confirmation.

**Deployed**: no schema change, so nothing to push — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` (no new or modified files). Code pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `77090fb`); confirmed via `vercel inspect --logs` that the new Production deployment (`dpl_BrcEab3kZw743rvWQkUFJyAAo1Ve`) cloned `Branch: main, Commit: 77090fb`, built, and went Ready, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` among others (`.vercel/project.json`'s linked `projectId`, project `mef-platform`, org `mef-wellness`). Confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com/checkin` and `/checkin/evening` both return a normal 307-to-`/login` (served by Vercel, routes exist and are protected, not error pages). Not verified: the logged-in experience on this specific production URL — the browser session driven this pass was against the local dev server, not production; only routing/redirect behavior was confirmed live.
